A live security-incident revoke chain confirmed effective — the first real rehearsal-history entry for `docs/runbooks/incident-response.md` itself. **Major outage and provider failure remained tabletop-only**, disclosed — no Docker, no reachable Supabase services, and CargoGrid's own architecture has no second infrastructure vendor to fail over to. >
> **Tier C review (4 independent adversarial lenses against commit `9c476b5`) found 7 more real gaps, all corrected directly in the runbooks themselves — not application/database code, none of which changed this checkpoint — since a false safety claim or an incomplete procedure in a runbook is itself a live hazard, not merely a disclosure gap.**

Also affects legal-hold-on-files and 6 append-only ledger/lineage tables. Registered `ISS-2026-265`/`HDN-BLK-034` (High).
>
> **(2) The headline finding: `app.revoke_all_actor_sessions`'s own session-status flip is never consulted by any RLS policy, RPC, or `app.evaluate_permission` anywhere in this codebase — pure bookkeeping with zero enforcement effect**, contradicting `incident-response.md`'s own stated mechanism. Registered `ISS-2026-264`/`HDN-BLK-035` (High); resolution guidance in `disaster-recovery.md` §4 item 2 re-ordered to lead with the actually-enforced mechanism.

Only edge construction changed; the `covered` recursive CTE's own multi-hop transitive-closure walk (a function covered because it calls a helper that calls another helper that finally reaches `evaluate_permission`/`assert_actor_is_session_identity`) is byte-identical, so the invariant proved is unweakened.

Original: **692,092.8ms** (~11.5 minutes). Rewrite: **556.4ms**. Verdicts: byte-identical (both empty). **Speedup: 1244×.** Full 229-file `bash scripts/db-tests/run.sh` re-run clean afterward, `ALL PASSED`.

> **Four independent parallel investigation lenses covered the full charter**: IP-restriction route-handler wiring feasibility; `app.create_integration_connection`'s own remaining step-up-MFA gap scoping; an OWASP-style abuse-pattern sweep (SQL injection, IDOR, CSRF, XSS, rate limiting, API-key handling, webhook signature verification, file-upload validation, prompt injection/AI-governed-action approval, open redirect); and the remaining infra items (extension relocation, dependency scan, secrets/logs redaction, runbook drafting).
>
> **The checkpoint's own single highest-priority item, `ISS-2026-150` (High, "must not be deferred again"), is `RESOLVED`.** `app.assert_ip_allowed`/`app.has_active_ip_allowlist_bypass` (real and correct since Prompt 356 but with zero real callers anywhere in the mutation layer) are now composed into all 4 platform-default high-risk functions (`decide_ai_output_approval`, `activate_enterprise_idp_connection`, `approve_mfa_exception`, `create_integration_connection`), each gaining a trailing `p_client_ip text default null` parameter, scope `'admin'`, exempting a null IP or an active bypass-grant holder.
>
> **A genuine implementation defect was found and fixed before this checkpoint's own first commit, not shipped.** A first `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` draft silently created a second, co-existing overload per function instead of replacing it — Postgres does not treat an appended trailing parameter as a signature match for `CREATE OR REPLACE` — which would have left every existing caller permanently bound to the OLD, un-gated version forever, exactly the "cosmetic partial wire-up" this issue's own disclosure explicitly forbade. Caught live against a real disposable database during this checkpoint's own Tier A validation, before any commit; corrected to an explicit `DROP FUNCTION` + `CREATE FUNCTION` + re-`GRANT EXECUTE` for all 4 functions, re-verified (exactly one overload each, correct grants, 11 db-test files clean end to end).
>
> **`ISS-2026-151` (the sibling step-up-MFA gap on the same function) was re-scoped precisely and deliberately deferred again.** 43 real call sites (not the "40+" estimate), 39 needing the gate, collapsible into 27 distinct step-up sequences across the same 16 files — stacking this onto the same checkpoint as the IP-restriction migration would exceed the normal bounded-repair budget and repeat the "rushed, under-tested wide edit" risk `CG-S14-IAE-039` already declined once for the same function.
>
> **Extension relocation corrected and partially resolved.** `pg_trgm`/`btree_gist` relocated out of `public` (2 of the item's own 8 named advisories); `postgis` found structurally non-relocatable (`relocatable = false` — a forced `DROP EXTENSION CASCADE` would destroy 15 live `geography`-typed columns across 12 real tables) — the matrix's own "clears 7 of 8" framing corrected to "clears 2 of 8", the remaining 6 (including the one ERROR advisory) registered separately (`ISS-2026-234`, owner a dedicated future task).
>
> **OWASP-style abuse sweep: 9 of 10 categories held, 1 Medium finding fixed.** SQL injection, IDOR (4 domains), CSRF, XSS, rate limiting, API-key handling, webhook signature verification, file-upload validation, and AI-governed-action human-approval integrity all held with zero findings, each live-forced against a real disposable database. The 10th (open redirect) surfaced a real, latent control-character-injection bypass in `lib/auth/redirect-allowlist.ts` — not currently exploitable (its one live caller constrains input upstream) but a genuine gap in a documented general-purpose control — found and fixed the same checkpoint (`ISS-2026-233`).
>
> **`ISS-2026-168` (service-role import boundary) resolved** via a new ESLint `no-restricted-imports` rule scoped to an explicit allowlist of the 27 real, currently-legitimate importers (this repository has no directory/filename convention that reliably distinguishes Client from Server Components).
>

Live-forced: a generically-held file could still be physically destroyed via a raw `service_role` DELETE, with no exception raised and no audit event captured — directly falsifying the first round's own claim that the bridge "closes both directions at once." Fixed by computing the combined native-or-generic hold state and gating on it (`ISS-2026-226`).

Discarded rather than shipped broken, registered instead (`ISS-2026-231`, Medium, owner `HDN-386`) — mirrors `HDN-374`'s own Finding-2 and `HDN-375`'s own `finance_subledger_batches` self-correction precedent exactly.
>

> **Standout finding, Critical, fixed.** `app.verify_third_party_provider_webhook_signature` (inbound third-party GPS webhook gate) returned SQL NULL, not false, for a null signature — PL/pgSQL's `if not verify_...()` silently treats a NULL condition as false-not-met, so a fully unsigned webhook was accepted as genuine. Live-forced: a direct call with `p_signature => null` — no HMAC secret known at all — inserted a real telemetry report attributed to a real vehicle, `ingest_status = 'ok'`. `anon`-reachable directly via Supabase PostgREST, entirely bypassing the Next.js route's own app-layer signature-presence check. Directly violated Prompt 376 §24 ("unsigned callbacks fail"). This exact defect class had already been found and fixed twice in two later, structurally identical capabilities and never backported. **Fixed** by mirroring their own proven guard (`ISS-2026-209`). The identical latent defect in `app.verify_webhook_signature` (not currently live-exploitable, zero live caller) also fixed for consistency (`ISS-2026-210`, High).
>

>
> **`ISS-2026-147` item 1 closed.** 9 REST `/v1` route handlers had zero dedicated HTTP-layer test coverage. `authorizeApiV1Request()` internally constructs a real, non-injectable Supabase client and this environment has no local PostgREST/Supabase stack — built a shared, reusable fetch-stubbing test harness instead (`tests/api/v1/support/rpc-fetch-stub.ts`), stubbing `globalThis.fetch` at the exact network boundary `.rpc()` calls resolve to; every route's own real request parsing/validation/response-shaping logic still runs for real. 44 new tests across all 9 routes. `HARDENING_MATRIX.md` §7's own GraphQL wording corrected — live-forced confirmation that no GraphQL surface exists in this repository at all.

Finding 2's own new `app.issue_finance_invoice` guard had no backing constraint and did not survive genuine concurrency — live-forced with a real two-process race (two approved invoices from two distinct handoffs on one job order, issued concurrently) to still double-bill, disproving the guard's own claim to close `ISS-2026-195` under concurrency; **fixed** with a real backing partial unique index, `finance_invoices_job_order_issued_unique on (tenant_id, job_order_id) where status = 'issued'`, structurally impossible to violate regardless of concurrency. `app.request_finance_settlement_reversal` bypassed fiscal period lock entirely — live-forced: a posted settlement in a subsequently-LOCKED period was still successfully reversed by an ordinary FIN:Approve holder — **fixed**, mirroring `app.post_finance_settlement`'s own established check.

> **One additive migration** (`20260811000000_harden_financial_integrity_invoicing_and_idempotency.sql`). **Four independent parallel investigation lenses** (revenue chain; cost/AP chain; period lock/reversal/concurrency/rounding; loyalty liability/payroll/tax/statutory), each required to live-force its own findings on disposable databases rather than accept a code read as proof. Cost/AP chain, payment/journal reconciliation, period lock, reversal, correction, rounding, tax snapshotting, payroll-handoff aggregation, and RPD-016's statutory gates all held clean.
>
> **`ISS-2026-194` (High) — quote-level tax silently double-applied at invoicing.** `app.job_orders.revenue_snapshot` always carries both `subtotalAmount` (pre-tax) and `totalAmount` (tax-inclusive) whenever the originating quotation applied a line-level `tax_pct`. `app.prepare_finance_invoice_from_readiness` read `totalAmount` and treated it as the invoice's own pre-tax subtotal, so a caller also passing `p_tax_code` (the normal path — every existing db-test fixture exercising this function does) had `app.calculate_finance_tax` apply the same rate a second time. Live-forced: a quote (qty=3, unit_price=333333.33, `tax_pct`=11) yielded `subtotal=999999.99 tax=110000.00 total=1109999.99` at the quote level; invoicing doubled it to `tax_amount=122100.00 total_amount=1232099.99` — an ~11% overcharge. **Fixed** by reading `subtotalAmount` instead.
>
> **`ISS-2026-195` (High) — a job order could reach `issued` on two full-amount invoices from two distinct handoffs, doubling the bill.** Live-forced: a job with real revenue 3,000,000 was invoiced twice via two handoffs, both `issued`, 6,000,000 billed for 3,000,000 of work. **The first fix draft was self-corrected before commit, not a Tier C finding**: it gated `app.prepare_finance_invoice_from_readiness` itself (deny a second DRAFT invoice per job order). Live-force verifying against the full 229-file suite before committing showed this broke a sanctioned flow — `finance-invoice.sql`'s own existing discard-boundary fixture prepares a second draft invoice from a second, later handoff on the same job order, and `20260728140000_create_operations_billing_readiness.sql` explicitly discloses "this migration does not forbid a legitimate re-handoff after a later reevaluation" (OPS-181). The shipped fix instead gates `app.issue_finance_invoice` — the actual AR/GL posting boundary — denying a second `issued` invoice per job order while leaving draft/submitted/approved states freely creatable and discardable exactly as OPS-181 intends.
>
> **Closes `ISS-2026-162`'s Finance/HRIS-Payroll scope (Medium)** — 10 functions total (the 6 originally named by `HDN-371`/`373` plus 4 more this checkpoint's own wider sweep found sharing the identical unguarded check-then-insert race: `app.post_finance_subledger_batch`, `app.create_and_post_finance_system_journal`, `app.import_finance_bank_statement`, `app.stage_finance_exchange_rate_import`) fixed with the codebase's own already-proven "design note 9(a)" exception-handler pattern. 2 representative mechanisms live-forced with a genuine two-process race each (`app.prepare_finance_journal_reversal`, `app.post_finance_subledger_batch`), both now return the winner's row gracefully instead of a raw `unique_violation`, committed as permanent regression tests. **The 3 non-Finance functions that finding also named (Commercial, WMS, Platform/Auth), plus `ISS-2026-163` (`app.prepare_job_order`'s own defective exception handler, same function family), are explicitly out of this checkpoint's Financial Integrity charter — handed to `HDN-387`, not silently dropped.**
>
> **`ISS-2026-196` (Medium) — `app.run_loyalty_expiry_sweep`'s own `p_as_of` parameter was silently ignored.** Both primitives it composes (`app.expire_loyalty_point_lots`/`app.expire_loyalty_benefit_entitlements`) hardcoded `clock_timestamp()` and accepted no as-of argument at all — live-forced: a lot due in 2 days, swept with `p_as_of` 3 days into the future, was silently left untouched. **Fixed** by adding an optional trailing `p_as_of` parameter to both, threaded through from `run_loyalty_expiry_sweep`'s own `v_as_of`.
>

> **Eight migrations, nine independent findings, all live-forced before being fixed rather than fixed on the sweep's say-so. Headline: `app.evaluate_permission` (the root RBAC gate, ~1,124 callers) never checked tenant membership at all** — `app.tenant_user_identities`/`app.has_active_tenant_membership` and `app.role_assignments` are separate tables with no FK/trigger cascade, and `app.revoke_auth_identity` touches only the former, so a genuinely revoked ex-member retained every role-based permission indefinitely. Live-confirmed end to end: a revoked user, acting as themselves (no forged actor — a revocation-propagation gap, not an identity-forgery one), successfully called both a read and a write RPC gated by `evaluate_permission`, which returned `allowed=true, reason='role_grant'`. **Fixed** at `supabase/migrations/20260810300000_harden_rbac_evaluator_tenant_membership_check.sql` (`ISS-2026-180`) — a self-caught regression (the new internal call needed its own `service_role` grant, since Postgres checks `EXECUTE` at the call site before the security context switches) was found and fixed before it ever reached a committed state.
>
> **Largest finding of Step 15 to date: the entire Finance manual/period/config/import-export write surface — 95 functions plus `app.enqueue_job` — was `SECURITY INVOKER` instead of `SECURITY DEFINER`, and has been completely unreachable by any real `authenticated` session since it shipped.** 76 top-level entry points, each already carrying a real, deliberate `authenticated` grant, plus the 19 `check_finance_*_authority` helpers. Live-forced: a genuine, non-superuser Finance Manager session calling `app.create_finance_journal_draft` for their own tenant was refused `permission denied for table permissions`, three call frames inside `evaluate_permission` itself — because `SECURITY INVOKER` means every nested call keeps executing as `authenticated`, which holds no grant on `app.permissions`, the Finance authority helpers, or DML on `app.finance_journals` and its siblings. A first fix draft (granting the missing intermediate helpers directly to `authenticated`) was live-tested and abandoned — it left the outer entry point's own table `INSERT`/`UPDATE` still failing and permanently exposed `evaluate_permission` to direct calls. The working fix converts only the true entry points to `SECURITY DEFINER`, proven sufficient because every nested call then executes as that entry point's owner. **Fixed** at `supabase/migrations/20260810700000_harden_finance_authority_chain_security_definer.sql` (`HDN-BLK-015`/`ISS-2026-182`, 95 functions, mechanically generated from live `pg_get_functiondef` output, diffed byte-identical elsewhere), together with `ISS-2026-183` (`create_and_post_finance_system_journal` had no authority check of its own — found by the fix's own verification, when restoring reachability tripped the pre-existing `ATW-032` sweep) and, at `supabase/migrations/20260810800000_harden_finance_journal_view_gate_and_self_approval.sql`: `ISS-2026-184` (`finance_journals`/`finance_journal_lines` RLS bypassed `FIN:View` entirely — a zero-permission tenant member read every journal row directly via RLS — fixed mirroring the already-shipped `payroll_periods_select_scoped` precedent) and `ISS-2026-181` (Finance journal self-approval, sharing `ISS-2026-139`'s exact shape — explicitly flagged mid-investigation that it had to land together with the reachability fix, not before it, or a High-severity gap would open the moment the feature became callable again). Fixing the self-approval guard broke 4 pre-existing Finance test files' own setup fixtures (single-actor draft→post lifecycles), each corrected to use a second, genuinely distinct `FIN:Approve` holder rather than worked around.
>
> **Carried forward from `HDN-372` and closed**: `HDN-BLK-012`/`ISS-2026-165` — 13 dashboard functions sharing `HDN-BLK-011`'s actor-forgery shape, fixed at `supabase/migrations/20260810200000_harden_dashboard_actor_identity_gaps.sql`; 5 of the 13 additionally gate a field-masking entitlement on the same forged parameter — a genuine unmasking bypass, not merely a record-scope widening. `HDN-BLK-014`/`ISS-2026-179` — of ~30 candidates, every one's actual call sites were grepped first: 16 confirmed terminal/self-referential and fixed at `supabase/migrations/20260810400000_harden_crm_ops_actor_identity_gaps.sql`; ~14 confirmed genuinely called with third-party actor arguments elsewhere in the schema and **not** fixed — an unconditional assert would break those legitimate uses, so they are re-registered narrower as `ISS-2026-186`, needing a per-call-site audit rather than a blind repository-wide edit. `ISS-2026-171`/`173` — own-row RLS gaps on `notifications`/`saved_report_views` post-revocation, fixed at `supabase/migrations/20260810500000_harden_own_row_rls_membership_gap.sql`, plus one further instance found in the same migration (`ISS-2026-185`, `notification_preferences`, identical shape).
>
> **`ISS-2026-139` (loyalty redemption maker/checker collapse, seeded from `HARDENING_MATRIX.md` §4)** — live-forced exactly as described (a "Redemption Clerk" role holding only `LYL:Edit`, explicitly not `Configure`, walked two unrelated accounts' real points into real vouchers in one session, zero second reviewer) and **fixed** at `supabase/migrations/20260810600000_harden_loyalty_redemption_maker_checker.sql`. **`ISS-2026-137`** (Supreme-Admin-override gap) independently re-verified — the table is actually MORE locked down than its siblings (missing both the append-only trigger and any `UPDATE`/`DELETE` grant at all); existing disposition (`OPEN`, Low, "no live vulnerability, only a missing correction path") confirmed accurate, no change made.
>
> **Six further findings registered, not fixed, each with a named forward owner**: `ISS-2026-186` (~14 residual shared RBAC primitives, owner a future checkpoint); `ISS-2026-187`/`188` (support-access reauth/session-end gaps, owner `HDN-378`); `ISS-2026-189` (Finance-adjacent `employees` column-grant, judgment call); `ISS-2026-190` (a sibling of `ISS-2026-184`'s exact shape on `performance_calibration_adjustments_select_scoped`, out of this checkpoint's Finance scope); `ISS-2026-191` (doc-drift); `ISS-2026-192` (`principal_memberships` self-record variant, likely by design).
>

> **Found and fixed at the root, same checkpoint: `HDN-BLK-011`/`ISS-2026-164`.** Four independent parallel adversarial investigations (DB/RLS/grants; API/service layer; storage/jobs/cache/reports; support/AI/webhooks/audit), each with its own live two-tenant fixture. The `ATW-032` hardening pass's own "STABLE/IMMUTABLE reads are exempt" premise is false for a `SECURITY DEFINER` function — it bypasses RLS, so a forged actor is exactly what lets a caller read what they could not otherwise. **9 functions, live-forced or clearly code-verified**: `app.get_self_employee` (**live-forced** — full employee PII, including columns deliberately excluded from the table's own column-restricted grant, read cross-tenant by a non-member attacker); `app.resolve_customer_owner_account_scope` (**live-forced**, the shared root of the 10-function `ATW-023` customer-inventory-access family — fixing this one root propagates protection to all 10 without touching them, live-reverified transitively); `app.query_audit_logs`/`app.export_audit_logs` (**live-forced**, missed by `ATW-032`'s own candidate regex since they name the parameter `p_requester_auth_user_id`); `app.list_notifications_for_recipient`/`app.count_unread_notifications` (**live-forced** — the pre-existing check is trivially bypassed by forging both parameters to the same victim identity); `app.get_workflow_instance_history` (**live-forced**); `app.get_approval_request_history`/`app.get_shipment_status_history` (code-verified, identical shape).
>
> **This checkpoint's own Tier C security/tenant review then ran a wider, independently live-tested closure sweep and found 4 more functions of the identical shape** — `app.get_notification_preferences`, `app.get_custom_field_values`, `app.list_pending_approval_steps_for_actor`, `app.resolve_actor_owner_account_scope` (two direct siblings of already-fixed functions, in the same migration files) — **fixed in the same checkpoint**, this lane's own charter, in a second migration. **13 functions fixed directly, 24 protected total** (corrected from an original miscounted 9/12/21).

Also at Tier C: the standing `ATW-032` sweep's own regex blind spot for `p_requester_auth_user_id` was broadened (closing the class, not just its 2 known instances), and the new regression gate was rewritten position-aware (was a bare substring match). > 1. **`HDN-BLK-007` / `ISS-2026-158` (High)** — `scripts/git/check-worktree-collision.test.ts:40` asserts the current branch has commits ahead of `origin/main`; a CI checkout of `main` **is** `origin/main`, so it can never pass. Because that `Test` step runs first, **six governance steps report `skipped` and have never executed in CI on a push** — including the **secret scan** and the **dependency vulnerability audit** (a seventh, protected-path check, is PR-only-gated and absent from any push regardless), the very gate `ISS-2026-007` added after a broken `pnpm audit` hid 20 real advisories, 11 high, for an entire phase.
> 2. **`HDN-BLK-008` / `ISS-2026-159`** — the `db` job's concurrency proofs call `pg_read_file('/tmp/…')`, which reads the **server's** filesystem; in CI Postgres is a Docker service container with its own `/tmp`.
> 3. **`HDN-BLK-009` / `ISS-2026-160`** — the `e2e` job sets no environment, so `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL` is unset and guarded routes 500 where specs assert `< 500`. This also exposes a real product question: several specs are *named* for failing safe on exactly this condition, while the code throws at env validation instead.

CPL-321 (Redemption Approval and Fulfillment): composes CPL-320's stock reservation, CPL-318's points-consumption, and CPL-319's entitlement-issuance at the moment of redemption; resolves a genuine dual-authority design tension via `app.submit_loyalty_redemption` always creating a real `pending_approval` intent record first, then attempting inline composition that only succeeds synchronously for a caller independently holding `LYL:Edit`; builds genuinely new `physical_item`/`service_credit` fulfillment tracking. CPL-322 (Expiry and Fraud Prevention): a real, storm-controlled `app.jobs`-backed expiry-sweep RPC mirroring HRT-291's own batch-run shape, composing CPL-318/319's own expire functions; a governed fraud review-case workflow composing CPL-317's own hold/release RPCs (never a second, competing hold table); a suppression/cooldown mechanism mirroring HRT-291's own suppression shape exactly. CPL-323 (Liability Reconciliation Analytics): mirrors FIN-209's execute/resolve-then-certify reconciliation-gate shape as new Loyalty-owned objects; resolves a genuine no-fabricated-conversion-rate design question by reporting points liability as a raw, dimensionless figure, never blended with the four currency-denominated lines; every total recomputed live from raw ledger/event tables each run, formally closing the "Loyalty ledger exactness" evidence citation named at Phase 8 kickoff.
>
> Batch 5's own Tier C review (4 lenses, each live-testing against a fresh disposable database, seeded with 5 specific items the orchestrating session itself pre-flagged during its own solo-prompt spot-verification) found and fixed at root cause: a **Critical** finding, confirmed independently by all four lenses and matching the orchestrating session's own pre-review hypothesis exactly — `app.submit_loyalty_redemption`'s inline auto-approve composition attempt caught only `insufficient_authority`-prefixed exceptions and re-raised every other failure (a routine, guaranteed-to-occur "reward out of stock" condition, not only a misconfigured reward), aborting the whole transaction and silently destroying the just-inserted `pending_approval` redemption row and its event — directly contradicting the migration's own documented "every submission always creates the intent record" invariant — fixed by widening the catch to swallow every composition failure unconditionally, always preserving the row for a human to resolve via `app.decide_loyalty_redemption`. A second **Critical** finding, found only by the cross-prompt-integration lens — CPL-323's own `reward_fulfillment_liability_total` (the 5th, disclosed liability line) trusted `app.loyalty_redemptions.status` directly with no re-derivation from the append-only event log, unlike the other four liability lines in the same run — a direct, RPC-bypassing status corruption could silently understate certified liability with zero detection — fixed by re-deriving redemption status from its latest event type and adding a new `redemption_liability_status_mismatch` exception type. A **High** finding — CPL-323's own new db-test fixture UUID range genuinely collided with two pre-existing, unrelated files (`finance-cash-bank.sql`, `procurement-sourcing.sql`), breaking the full, unmodified `bash scripts/db-tests/run.sh` harness partway through, its own "grep-verified unclaimed" header claim false — fixed by renumbering to the batch's own next free `346xxx` range. A **Medium** finding — CPL-323's `app.get_customer_portal_loyalty_summary` claimed to compose CPL-319's own list function but instead ran an independent, duplicate raw aggregation — fixed to genuinely call the composed function. A **Low/Medium** defense-in-depth finding — 5 of CPL-320's own version-checked reward-lifecycle functions relied solely on the terminal `UPDATE`'s own repeated version predicate for NULL-bypass safety, missing the explicit up-front rejection every other Batch 5 sibling already carries — fixed by adding the identical explicit prefix to all 5 functions. Two items the orchestrating session flagged were confirmed correct with no fix needed: the CPL-322 fraud hold genuinely, live-provenly blocks a new CPL-321 redemption attempt; CPL-320/322's own dual `'loyalty_expiration'`/`'loyalty_expiry_sweep'` `job_type` literals are adequately disclosed and the dead placeholder is never dispatched anywhere. One pre-existing, unrelated defect was discovered while re-running the full harness post-fix: `scripts/db-tests/hris-shift-roster-scheduling.sql` (100% Phase 7/HRIS scope, untouched by this session) belongs to the already-registered day-of-week/wall-clock-dependent fixture-flakiness class — registered as `ISS-2026-135`, not fixed, out of this review's own charter.

A **High**-class NULL-bypass optimistic-concurrency defect across 3 functions spanning CPL-315/316/317 (`record_version <> p_expected_version` silently evaluates falsy-NULL when the expected version itself is NULL) — fixed by repeating the version predicate on the `UPDATE` itself in every affected function. The identity-model rework HRT-286's own review diagnosed was implemented in full: `app.tickets.requester_employee_id` relaxed to nullable, a new nullable `requester_customer_account_id` (FK `app.accounts`) added with a real structural `tickets_requester_identity_shape` CHECK enforcing exactly one, matching `channel`; `channel` widened to `('internal','customer')`; `app._create_ticket` given a real `p_channel` parameter (old overload dropped, never left stale); the three `INNER JOIN app.employees` reads converted to `LEFT JOIN`, live-tested with real customer requesters carrying zero `app.employees` row; identity resolution **generalized, not duplicated**, into one new `app._is_ticket_requester_party` dispatch helper reused across every authority function, reusing the established Layer-4 primitives (`app.resolve_customer_owner_account_scope`, `app.actor_holds_customer_user_layer`) rather than inventing a parallel mechanism — exactly the shape the immediately-preceding Tier C review recommended. HRT-286's own internal-channel db-test re-ran byte-for-byte unchanged, 15/15. Six new customer-safe RPCs are their own deliberately narrower projections (no queue/assignee identity, internal notes structurally unreachable); account/company/site all map onto the existing `app.accounts` row shape (no new table), with deliberately no implicit parent-to-child access cascade. Gate suite fresh after Implement: `typecheck` 0 errors; `lint` 0/215; `pnpm run test` 3964/3964; `next build` clean (2 new routes); `db:test` full harness ALL PASSED end-to-end for the first time this session (outside the disclosed `ISS-2026-059` time band).
>
> **The subsequent Tier C review — 4 parallel adversarial lenses, one with a dedicated mandate to independently re-derive all 7 of `ISS-2026-085`'s own verification questions live rather than trust the resolution claim — confirmed the identity-model fix is genuine and found 2 further CONFIRMED findings, both HIGH, unrelated to the identity-model work itself: 0 CRITICAL, 2 HIGH, 1 LOW, 0 rejected** (full detail `docs/build-log/phase-07/HRT-287.md` §14). **HIGH**: `app.get_customer_ticket` returned `resolution_summary`/`cancelled_reason`/`last_reopen_reason` to the customer verbatim regardless of who authored the text — live-reproduced twice (a staff-authored cancel and a staff-authored reopen, each containing an internal-only rationale, e.g. "customer flagged high churn risk in CRM, do not offer refund") both leaked straight to the customer's own ticket view through the ordinary, unmodified staff UI, with zero staff-facing warning that the field was about to cross the tenant boundary. Fixed with genuine authorship tracking: `resolution_summary` (unconditionally staff-authored by construction, since no transition-authority rule ever admits a customer into `resolved`) is now never exposed to the customer projection at all (registered as `ISS-2026-088`, Low, a disclosed residual scope-narrowing rather than a future feature gap); `cancelled_reason`/`last_reopen_reason` are genuinely dual-authored (the same generic RPC services both customer self-service and staff-driven actions on the customer's behalf), so a new pair of tracking columns records which party actually authored the text and only the customer's own words are ever echoed back. **HIGH**: `app._create_ticket`'s customer-channel idempotency match (and the underlying partial unique index) omitted `requester_customer_account_id` — live-reproduced both sequentially and under genuine concurrent OS processes: a customer_user actor legitimately scoped to multiple accounts who reused one idempotency key across two different-account requests with matching content got silently short-circuited to the first account's ticket, with the second request's own account never receiving a ticket at all, no error — fixed by adding the account id to both the index and the match/replay logic. **1 LOW**: a self-report file-count discrepancy between the completion report and the `TASK_LEDGER.md` row, corrected.
>
> Both HIGH findings fixed at root cause via one new additive migration (`20260731090000`; `20260731080000` left untouched). **Gate suite independently re-run a third time by the orchestrating session, every number matching**: `typecheck` 0 errors; `lint` 0 errors/215 warnings (unchanged); `pnpm run test` **3964/3964**; `next build` clean, both routes present; `git:check-paths`/`security:check`/`security:audit`/`data-classification:check`/`threat-model:check`/`standards:check`/`docs:check`/`git:check` all clean; `pnpm run db:test` **ALL PASSED**, the full official harness end to end (ran outside the `ISS-2026-059` failure band). The orchestrating session directly read the corrective migration and confirmed both fixes are genuinely present and correctly shaped: the `cancelled_reason_authored_by_customer`/`last_reopen_reason_authored_by_customer` tracking columns genuinely gate what `get_customer_ticket` echoes back (and `resolution_summary` is unconditionally nulled), and `requester_customer_account_id` is genuinely part of both the rebuilt unique index and the `_create_ticket` match logic — never accepted on the fix agent's self-report alone.

> **The subsequent Tier C review — 4 parallel adversarial lenses — found 8 CONFIRMED findings: 0 CRITICAL, 2 HIGH, 3 MEDIUM, 3 LOW, 0 rejected** (full detail `docs/build-log/phase-07/HRT-286.md` §12). **The headline finding, independently and identically caught by BOTH the spec-compliance and cross-prompt-integration lenses (HIGH, foundation-severity)**: the migration's own header and the implementer's own completion report both claimed "channel is a pure additive CHECK-widen, never a redesign" for Prompts 287 (customer) and 288 (helpdesk) — **this does not hold**. `app.tickets.requester_employee_id` is `NOT NULL`, FK'd exclusively to `app.employees`; every authority function (`can_access_ticket`, `is_ticket_staff`, `_ticket_transition_authority`) resolves identity only through the employee-only `app.get_self_employee`; `app._create_ticket` — the claimed "shared engine" — has no `p_channel` parameter at all; three read RPCs `INNER JOIN app.employees` on the requester. Confirmed directly against the real `287_CUSTOMER_TICKET_PROMPT.md`/`288_CARGOGRID_HELPDESK_PROMPT.md` briefs already in the repo: both require a genuinely new, non-employee requester/staffing principal (Layer 4 customer; CargoGrid support-session-grant-based staffing) the current NOT NULL/FK/INNER-JOIN/employee-only shape cannot represent without real expand-and-contract schema work — relaxing the NOT NULL constraint, adding nullable discriminator column(s), converting those INNER JOINs to LEFT JOINs, and adding new authority-function branches. The narrower part of the claim IS true (the `channel` CHECK constraint itself is a trivial, well-precedented DROP/ADD, and the RLS policy objects don't need to change, only the functions they call) — so this is real, scoped, disclosed rework, not a full redesign. **Correctly disposed as a disclosure, not a forced fix** (`ISS-2026-085`, High, OPEN) — resolving it is a genuine architectural decision belonging to whichever session picks up Prompt 287, outside a single-prompt review-fix batch's own mandate; the migration's own object comments were corrected in place so a future implementer reading the live schema sees the real scope, not the original overclaim. **1 further HIGH, fixed**: `transition_ticket_status` checked the transition-graph/authority rules BEFORE `record_version`, unlike every sibling versioned-write RPC in the same file — live-reproduced with two genuine concurrent processes, the race loser got a misleading `invalid_transition` instead of `stale_version` (data stayed correct; error class was wrong and order-dependent) — fixed by reordering to match every sibling function. **3 MEDIUM**: `redacted_reason`/`removed_reason` (both staff-only rationale text) were readable via raw SELECT by, respectively, the redacted message's own requester and any tenant employee — live-reproduced, fixed with the established PLT-114 column-restricted-grant pattern; `TKT:Edit` grants blanket tenant-wide ticket-content access (read every internal note, reply, reclassify, transfer) with no per-queue scoping, in tension with the migration's own "reserved for configuration" claim — investigated, found to be a genuine, pre-existing intentional escape hatch elsewhere in the same migration (`redact_ticket_message` already relies on it, as does the db-test's own coverage), so disclosed rather than force-changed without a clear mandate (`ISS-2026-086`, Medium, OPEN). **3 LOW**: no attachment-upload UI form despite real, tested RPC/malware-scan support; no browser/E2E spec (repository-wide precedent); an id-existence-oracle gap on one queue-roster removal RPC, matching an already-accepted repository shape.
>

> **Implements**: a versioned KPI library/cycle/goal/scoring/calibration/appeal workflow (283, 13 tables) with a real k=5 cohort-suppression floor enforced server-side, not a UI mask, and a structural zero-write proof against any pay/employment/access table; a training catalogue/session/enrollment/certificate/development-plan/restricted-talent-succession capability (284, 19 tables) reusing the same k-anonymity discipline and PLT-131/132's durable job framework for certificate expiry; and a pure composition-layer ESS/MSS workspace (285, zero migration) that calls exclusively through HRT-274/278-284's own already-authority-checked RPCs, with a live-proven defensive team-id re-filter closing the one real dual-role (manager+HR) widening risk a naive composition would have introduced. **The C-24 unmasked-audit-log-leak defect class — which recurred in HRT-280, HRT-281, AND HRT-282, three checkpoints in a row — was independently verified genuinely NOT repeated here**: both 283 and 284 built the masking discipline in from the start (25 and 47 `capture_audit_event` call sites respectively, all reviewed, none passing a raw reason/score/rating).
>
> **The subsequent combined Tier C review — 4 parallel adversarial lenses — found 9 CONFIRMED findings: 1 CRITICAL, 3 HIGH, 3 MEDIUM/MEDIUM-HIGH, 2 LOW, 0 rejected** (full detail across `docs/build-log/phase-07/HRT-283.md`/`HRT-284.md`/`HRT-285.md`, each carrying the shared batch-review section). **CRITICAL, and independently the most consequential finding of this kind so far**: 7 read RPCs (5 in 283, 2 in 284) — `get_performance_cycle`, `list_performance_goal_progress_entries`, `list_performance_assessment_kpi_scores`, `get_performance_outcome`, `list_performance_calibration_adjustments`, `get_training_session`, `list_talent_pool_members` — raised `column reference "id" is ambiguous` on **every single call, unconditionally**, the exact recurring bare-`id`-vs-`RETURNS TABLE`-output-column defect class that has now hit six-plus prior checkpoints this phase, here at its most severe: these RPCs could never have returned a row in production. They survived both prompts' own Tier A/B because neither db-test fixture ever called these specific RPC names and the TypeScript unit tests mock the Supabase client entirely — a real, load-bearing gap in what "tested" meant for this slice, not a coverage nicety. Fixed via table-aliasing across all 7. **3 HIGH**: `app.enqueue_job`'s check-then-insert on `idempotency_key` leaked a raw, undiscriminated `23505` under genuine concurrency, live-reproduced with two real concurrent OS processes racing HRT-284's own certificate-expiry batch RPC — fixed at the shared primitive with a proper, carefully-discriminated exception handler; `app.attach_training_certificate_evidence` (284's own PLT-128 malware-scan reuse) had zero UI caller despite the Certificates section's own copy explicitly claiming the capability existed — wired end-to-end, copy corrected; the batch's own Tier A `rbac-enforcement.sql` ATW-032 gate was live-red at 3 functions (`acknowledge_performance_outcome`/`submit_performance_appeal`/`submit_performance_self_assessment`) — independently read and reproduced live against each function's own definition (genuine, correct self-scope equality checks the mechanical sweep doesn't credit, not a live authority gap) and closed via the sweep's own documented escape hatch with full written justification, never by weakening the gate. **3 MEDIUM/MEDIUM-HIGH fixed**: 2 RLS policies called the `service_role`-only `app.evaluate_permission` directly instead of the established `SECURITY DEFINER` wrapper, failing closed (no leak) but breaking raw-table SELECT for genuinely authorized actors; a real, tested reviewer-reassignment RPC (283's own decision 5 "mandatory reading" business rule) had its UI action `void`-ed rather than wired — fixed end-to-end; the MSS team roster/approval-queue silently truncated at 50/20 rows with no signal, contradicting §17's cursor-pagination expectation — the silent half fixed with a visible truncation banner, the full cursor-UI build disclosed as `ISS-2026-084` (Low). **2 LOW**: `EssHomeSummary.pendingLeaveRequestCount` was a hardcoded fake `0` on an otherwise-real contract — fixed, now genuinely computed; "provider" evidence-file privacy (§16's other named noun) was never built, only the certificate half — disclosed as `ISS-2026-083` (Low), copy corrected to stop overclaiming.

> Implements payroll calendar/period, versioned component/formula, employee component assignment, input-snapshot freeze, run/batch calculation, earning/deduction/benefit/tax/loan/reimbursement lines, exception review, PLT-123-routed maker-checker finalization, private payslips, and a reconciled Finance handoff — 13 payroll tables + 3 Finance-handoff tables, ~90 functions, across 3 migrations (`20260731000000` foundation, `20260731010000` Finance-handoff binding). **Finance-ownership boundary**: Payroll owns its full lifecycle end to end; the handoff (`app.payroll_finance_handoff_batches/_gl_lines/_payment_instructions`) is a new table family Payroll itself owns and populates via `app.prepare_finance_payroll_disbursement_handoff_from_payroll_run` (mirroring `app.prepare_finance_vendor_bill_from_actual_cost`'s exact shape), since no Finance-side capability to consume a payroll disbursement exists yet — zero `GRANT`/`INSERT`/`UPDATE` on any `app.finance_*`/`app.journal_*` table anywhere in either migration, structurally proven (not just claimed) via a live `pg_proc.prosrc` regex scan the checkpoint's own db-test performs. **`ISS-2026-074` resolved**: `app._resolve_payroll_time_inputs_for_period` reads both HRT-281's `app.payroll_time_inputs` and HRT-278's `app.attendance_sessions` with an explicit per-work-date precedence (timesheet-derived input authoritative where it exists; approved attendance contributes regular-only minutes elsewhere), live-proven crediting an attendance-only employee correctly. **RPD-016**: statutory components (PPh21, BPJS) seeded as `is_example_fixture=true`/`fixed_amount=0`, structurally un-approvable pending dated SME evidence — no real Indonesian statutory figure asserted anywhere. **RPD-023**: no per-action step-up MFA disclosed as a repository-wide, not Payroll-specific, pre-existing gap; compensating PLT-123 maker-checker (self-approval blocked on both finalize and reimbursement-decide) stands in. Compensation visibility is deliberately NOT derived from the general manager-scope helper every other HRT capability uses — `app.can_view_hris_payroll_row` is self-or-`HRS:View payroll` only, live-proven a direct manager with no explicit grant sees nothing. The implementing agent's own adversarial testing and Tier B walk found and fixed 6 defects before commit (2 RBAC-transitive-authority gaps caught live by the standing `rbac-enforcement.sql` gate itself, not manual review). Gate suite fresh after Implement: `typecheck` 0 errors; `lint` 0/205; `pnpm run test` 3755/3755; `db:test` ALL PASSED; `next build` clean (2 new routes).
>
> **The subsequent Tier C review — 4 parallel adversarial lenses, including genuinely concurrent OS `psql` processes — found 13 CONFIRMED, live-reproduced findings: 1 CRITICAL, 5 HIGH, 4 MEDIUM/MEDIUM-HIGH, 3 LOW, 0 rejected** (full detail `docs/build-log/phase-07/HRT-282.md` §13). **CRITICAL, and the single most severe finding of any Phase 7 checkpoint so far**: the security and integration lenses independently, separately confirmed the same defect — unmasked compensation amounts (reimbursement amounts, loan principal) and free-text decision/finalize/cancel reasons leaked into `app.audit_logs`, live-read by an ordinary `tenant_admin` holding **zero** payroll permission of any kind via `app.query_audit_logs`, despite that same actor being correctly denied on every one of the capability's own 16 tables and every read RPC. This is the third recurrence of this exact shape this phase (after HRT-280's leave/permit reasons and HRT-281's overtime/timesheet reasons) — and the worst, since this time it carried raw monetary figures tied to identifiable employees, not just free text. Fixed across 13 call sites in 12 functions by no longer routing sensitive reason/amount data into `capture_audit_event` at all (a disclosed, reasoned variant of HRT-281's own masked-projection-helper pattern, chosen because widening the shared `redact_audit_payload` key-name regex would silently change every other domain's audit trail); a new taxonomy class `C-24` was recorded for this now-3x-recurring shape. **5 HIGH, all fixed**: 3 real check-then-insert concurrency races (period/component/component-version creation), live-reproduced with genuinely concurrent OS processes leaking raw, undiscriminated `23505` to the loser; `freeze_payroll_period_inputs` had no advanced-run guard (unlike its sibling `reopen`), live-reproduced re-freezing a period out from under an in-flight run, blocked only incidentally by a raw FK `23503`; a timesheet-period/payroll-period boundary bug silently dropped an entire boundary-straddling week's real, HR-approved time from payroll with zero error or audit trace, live-reproduced end to end (480 real minutes → `regular_minutes=0`); the claimed durable-job/checkpoint mechanism was live-disproven — the whole job lifecycle runs inside one transaction, `app.claim_next_job`/`app.record_job_failure` are never called — the narrower, genuinely fixable part (mid-loop cancellation was unreachable dead code) was fixed with a new, correctly `HRS:Edit`-scoped RPC; the broader multi-transaction-resumability gap is honestly disclosed as `ISS-2026-079` (Medium, OPEN, architectural — also affects the pre-existing `roster_generation`/`leave_accrual` functions sharing this same shape). **4 MEDIUM/MEDIUM-HIGH fixed**: the 3 new Finance-handoff tables' RLS called the `service_role`-only `app.evaluate_permission` directly inside `USING (...)`, live-reproduced blocking even a genuinely-authorized `FIN:Edit` actor with a hard error (fail-closed, not a leak, but non-functional) — fixed with a `SECURITY DEFINER` wrapper matching the established pattern; a correction run could reference a finalized run from a *different* period with zero validation; idempotency replay omitted `currency`/`evidence_file_id`. **3 LOW**: the build log's own C-20 disclosure list was itself wrong (wrongly listed `cancel_payroll_run` as unwired when it is; omitted two genuinely-unreachable RPCs) — corrected; a contract doc-accuracy overclaim; a test-methodology observation (a broad `when others` handler could mask a real regression without failing the suite — independently re-verified the underlying property genuinely holds).

One recovery mid-batch: HRT-279's implementing agent completed its own work and staged it but ran out of context before its own commit step; HRT-280's agent found the staged diff, verified it was 279-only, and committed it separately as its own single commit before proceeding with its own chartered task — preserving the one-commit-per-prompt convention with zero work lost. Gate suite fresh after Implement: `typecheck` 0 errors; `lint` 0/205; `pnpm run test` 3672/3672; `db:test` ALL PASSED (195 migrations); `next build` clean (10 new routes).

> **The batch's own combined 4-lens Tier C review — against the merged 278-280 diff, one review covering all three prompts, not three separate ones — found 12 CONFIRMED, live-reproduced findings: 1 CRITICAL, 5 HIGH, 4 MEDIUM, 2 LOW, 0 rejected** (full detail in each capability's own build log §12). **Most severe (CRITICAL)**: `app.decide_leave_request`/`app.cancel_leave_request` delegated the employee lifecycle-status sync (`start_employee_leave`/`end_employee_leave`, HRT-274) using the CALLING actor's own authority, which hard-requires `HRS:Edit`. Live-reproduced breaking BOTH directions: a correctly-scoped Approve/Override-only approver was rejected approving a same-day leave, and an ordinary self-cancelling employee (zero HRS grants, self-service by design) was rejected cancelling their own already-started leave — both times rolling back the entire transaction, including the approval decision or the balance debit. Fixed by extracting a shared, authority-agnostic `app._transition_employee_leave_status` engine that the two governed entry points (`decide_leave_request`/`cancel_leave_request`) call directly, since their own authority was already established before ever reaching the transition; `start_employee_leave`/`end_employee_leave` remain unchanged, `HRS:Edit`-gated wrappers for their own direct callers. **3 further HIGH findings are the identical "raw-table overexposure bypasses careful RPC-layer scoping" shape recurring a THIRD time this phase** (after HRT-276's interview feedback/offer compensation and HRT-277's onboarding provisioning records): `attendance_sessions`/`attendance_events`, `schedule_assignments`, and `leave_requests`/`leave_balance_ledger` all carried tenant-membership-only RLS with an unrestricted `authenticated` grant, letting any zero-permission tenant member with no employee record at all read every employee's real clock-in/out timestamps, full shift roster, or leave/medical calendar via one trivial raw SELECT — while the RPC layer's own careful self/manager/HRS:View scoping correctly returned zero rows for the identical actor. Fixed uniformly with one new shared helper, `app.can_view_hris_person_scoped_row`, reused identically across all three capabilities' RLS policies rather than three separate bespoke fixes. **1 further HIGH**: leave-request evidence files were validated only at creation, never re-validated on draft-update or submit — live-reproduced accepting a cross-tenant, unscanned, wrong-record-type file all the way through to HR review — fixed with a shared `_validate_leave_evidence_file` called at both write paths. **1 further HIGH**: leave approval never re-triggered attendance-exception recalculation for the covered date, so a backdated or same-day approval left a stale, contradictory open exception — fixed bidirectionally (approve suppresses, cancel lets it reappear).
>
> **4 MEDIUM findings fixed**: a genuine schedule-reassignment race correctly serialized to exactly one winner but leaked a raw, uncaught Postgres constraint-violation message to the loser instead of a clean domain error; `list_leave_requests`' manager-scoping broke specifically when an explicit employee filter was supplied, diverging from the sibling HRT-278/279 list functions; `generate_roster_schedule_assignments` had no real idempotency key, so an identical retry silently superseded and recreated every draft assignment rather than no-op'ing; four attendance read RPCs raised a hard `ambiguous column` error on every call and every non-null pagination cursor — the exact recurring bare-`id`-vs-`RETURNS TABLE`-output-column bug this phase's own Tier B taxonomy walk exists to catch, confirmed present only in HRT-278 and absent from the sibling HRT-279/280 migrations. **2 LOW, documentation-only**: HRT-280's own self-disclosed propagation note claiming an identical bug existed in HRT-279's job-replay code didn't hold up under live-code verification (no live bug on that path, flagged so the fix pass didn't spend effort adding an unneeded guard); a wrong column name in HRT-280's own build-log prose, corrected.
>

> **Prior checkpoint (2026-08-09, branch `claude/lanjut-276-280-ygphnr`) — `CG-S12-HRT-005` (Prompt 277, Onboarding and Offboarding) `VERIFIED`. Second task of the "lanjut 276-280" operator authorization**, run as its own single-prompt Tier-C batch (§3.2 trigger 3: writes through Platform identity/access authority) — the first Phase 7 capability to exercise the governed candidate→employee→user linkage ADR-0023 Part B reserved for it, built directly on `CG-S12-HRT-004` (Recruitment/ATS, `VERIFIED`).
>
> Implements a versioned onboarding/offboarding case workspace (checklist template→version→task→dependency with publish-time cycle detection, idempotent case-start for both accepted-offer and direct-hire sources, per-task assignment/completion/waive/reopen with dependency-driven blocked state, asset/access/training/payroll/Operations handoff task types, PLT-123-routed finalize approval, cancellation, and a new `app.rehire_employee` lifecycle extension) — 9 tables, 32 functions. The ADR-0023 Part B conversion correctly reuses existing Platform primitives throughout rather than a second identity-writing path: `app.start_onboarding_case` calls HRT-274's `app.create_employee_draft`; access provisioning calls `app.invite_user`/`app.link_employee_user`/`app.assign_role`; access revocation calls `app.transition_user_status`. The implementing agent's own mandatory Tier B taxonomy walk (not a db-test failure) caught the checkpoint's own major self-found defect: `start_onboarding_case`'s idempotency replay originally matched on key alone, fixed with a full-tuple comparison wired into all 3 replay sites. 11 self-found defects fixed before commit in total. Gate suite fresh after Implement: `typecheck` 0 errors; `lint` 0/205; `pnpm run test` 3578/3578; `db:test` ALL PASSED; `next build` clean (5 new routes).
>
> **The subsequent Tier C review — 4 parallel adversarial lenses, live-tested against a real disposable Postgres — found the largest, most severe finding set of any Phase 7 checkpoint so far: 12 CONFIRMED, live-reproduced findings, 4 CRITICAL, 3 HIGH, 4 MEDIUM, 1 LOW, 0 rejected** (full detail `docs/build-log/phase-07/HRT-277.md` §12). **Most severe — a live-exploited privilege-escalation path**: `app.request_onboarding_access_provisioning` wrapped the previously service_role-only `app.assign_role` behind mere `HRS:Edit`, letting any ordinary HR/onboarding staffer grant ANY published tenant role — including cross-domain Finance/Procurement/Operations Approve/Override/Delete permissions — to themselves or any other tenant member. Live-reproduced twice against a fresh disposable database: an attacker holding only an ordinary "HR Ops" role (HRS Create/Edit/Export/View — no protected or administrative permission of any kind) granted a "Finance Controller" role (FIN Approve/Override/Delete, PRC Approve, OPS Override) to an unrelated tenant member, and separately to themselves, with `evaluate_permission` confirming the grant took effect immediately in both cases. **Second CRITICAL**: `app.cancel_onboarding_case` never revoked Platform access already granted by a concurrent or already-completed provisioning task — live-reproduced with two real concurrent `psql` sessions leaving a real, active role grant behind a cancelled case, the same "cancel-path doesn't cancel a dependent in-flight process" bug shape HRT-276 found on the approval-engine surface, recurring here on the access-provisioning surface. **Third CRITICAL**: the newly-added C-01 idempotency fix above was itself still incomplete for the `direct_hire` path — a same-key replay with a materially different hire (different name, employment type, email) silently returned the first case/employee with no conflict error. **Fourth CRITICAL**: access revocation was materially incomplete — the build log's own justification that `app.transition_user_status` "already cascades" role revocation was directly disproven live: its revoke branch touches only identity/membership tables, never `app.role_assignments`, and `app.evaluate_permission` never checks user/tenant-identity status at all — a "revoked" actor live-reproducibly retained full permission-gated write authority, successfully creating a brand-new onboarding case after being revoked.
>
> **All 4 CRITICAL plus 5 further findings fixed at root cause** via a new additive migration (`20260730890000`; `20260730880000` left untouched): the provisioning-grant path now requires `HRS:Override` (matching the revoke path's own established bar) plus a new `app.assert_actor_holds_role_version_permissions` delegation check — an actor may never grant, through this capability, a permission they do not themselves already hold in the target tenant; `cancel_onboarding_case` now revokes the case's own completed access grants before terminating; the idempotency-replay tuple widened to the full request payload across all 4 call sites; `request_onboarding_access_revocation` now explicitly revokes every active `role_assignments` row for the target identity, and now requires a non-empty reason (closing a 5th, HIGH finding — business rule 5's "emergency exit requires reason" was previously unenforceable on this exact RPC); a genuinely stranded-state task (deferred access grant against an already-revoked, never-reactivatable target) now rejects outright instead of falsely reporting completion; two unrestricted-grant findings on `onboarding_task_provisioning_requests`/`onboarding_case_events.notes` closed with column-restricted grants; a HIGH finding that handoff-task completion required no acknowledged evidence closed by requiring a note or file for `task_type='handoff'`. **2 findings disclosed as new-scope known issues rather than force-fixed**: `ISS-2026-072` (OPEN, High — the systemic PLT-110/112 gap beneath the revocation fix: `evaluate_permission` never consults user/tenant-identity status at all, a broader primitive-level design gap this one call site's fix cannot fully close on its own) and `ISS-2026-073` (OPEN, Medium — no "approved direct hire" precondition exists anywhere despite the prompt's own §21/§25 naming one as a distinct requirement from offer-acceptance).
>

> **Prior checkpoint (2026-08-09, branch `claude/lanjut-276-280-ygphnr`) — `CG-S12-HRT-004` (Prompt 276, Recruitment, Job Portal and ATS) `VERIFIED`. First task of a fresh, explicit "lanjut 276-280" operator authorization**, run as a single-prompt Tier-C batch (adaptive cap ≤3 inherited from HRT-275's own 2 Critical/High findings; independently cut to 1 by §3.2 trigger 2 — a first-of-its-kind-adjacent new anonymous/public entry point, the candidate job-application intake). Second Phase 7 capability build in the Recruitment/Workforce Entry-Exit workstream, built directly on `CG-S12-HRT-003` (Organization and Position Linkage, `VERIFIED`).
>
> Implements tenant-scoped recruitment end-to-end (approved vacancy → candidate → assessment → interview → offer) plus a genuinely public, anonymous, token-based, enumeration-safe, rate-limited job-application intake surface bound only to open vacancies (this repository's second anonymous entry point after PRC-251's vendor intake). 14 new tables, 55 functions. Candidates are deliberately NOT routed through `app.master_records` — purpose-bound recruitment data, not an operational actor identity (`HRT-276.md` §3.1) — and an accepted offer reaches `offer_accepted` while creating neither `app.employees` nor `app.users` rows, proven by an explicit db-test assertion; the governed candidate→employee→user conversion is Prompt 277's own chartered scope (ADR-0023 Part B). Public posting tokens are stored/returned directly rather than hashed, a disclosed divergence from the vendor-intake single-use-hash shape since a hash cannot support a multi-use, publicly-listed link. Offer approval reuses PLT-123 without a bespoke threshold table. Interview feedback is identity-gated to the assigned interviewer; duplicate-candidate detection never auto-merges; candidate PII is column-restricted from `authenticated` from day one (PLT-114's pattern, applied at first build, not retrofitted).
>
> Built via `Workflow`: Implement (one agent) → 4 parallel adversarial Tier C review lenses, live-tested against a real disposable Postgres (spec-compliance; security/RLS/tenant isolation; correctness/concurrency; cross-prompt integration) → one fix pass → independent gate re-run and direct code-read verification by the orchestrating session. The implementing agent's own honest adversarial `psql` testing found and fixed **12 real defects before commit** (disclosed as self-found, `HRT-276.md` §7): a systemic tenant-membership-fold gap (C-05) across ~30+ functions was the major one, plus a public-posting-token design flaw, an idempotency/duplicate-conflict conflation, a redundant status guard, an invalid `group by`, a recurring ambiguous-bare-column-reference bug (the same class HRT-274 found three times), a 3-bug chain in duplicate-candidate search, two `ATW-032` gate failures, a UI dead-action defect (`ApprovalStepForm` rewritten from a non-functional placeholder to real Approve/Reject buttons), and others.

> **Tier C's four lenses found 7 CONFIRMED, live-reproduced findings, all fixed**: **HIGH** — the RPC layer's filters/search/cursor pagination had zero UI caller and saved views hardcoded an empty filter payload, defeating their own purpose — fixed by wiring real `searchParams`-driven controls into the page and threading them into the saved-view actions. **HIGH** — `app.procurement_dashboard_saved_views`' RLS policy had no tenant predicate, only owner-check, combined with a blanket table grant — live-reproduced as a genuine cross-tenant leak via one auth identity linked to two tenants reading both tenants' saved views through a raw table select — fixed by revoking the direct grant entirely (real reads already go through owner-scoped RPCs). **HIGH** — a genuine concurrent-idempotency race on report export raised a raw, uncaught `unique_violation` instead of returning the existing run, live-reproduced with two real `psql` sessions — fixed with an exception-recovery block mirroring an already-correct sibling pattern in the same migration. **HIGH** — both cursor-paginated list RPCs silently, permanently dropped rows sharing an exact `created_at` tie at a page boundary — live-reproduced as total data loss — fixed with a composite `(created_at, id)` keyset cursor. **HIGH** — two summary RPCs' LATERAL joins omitted `tenant_id`, forcing full table scans of `vendor_kpi_scorecards`/`vendor_kpi_metric_values` at scale — live `EXPLAIN` at a 25-tenant/150-vendor synthetic scale confirmed Seq Scan before, Index Scan after — fixed by adding the tenant predicate. **HIGH + MEDIUM** — two dashboard summary RPCs projected `purchase_orders.currency`/`vendor_rate_versions.currency` unmasked to any `PRC:View`-only caller, diverging from PRC-260/COM-149's own already-`VERIFIED` cost masking of these exact columns — fixed by masking behind the same `PRC:View cost` gate, grouping on the masked expression so currency diversity isn't leaked via row count either. **One further defect caught by the orchestrating session's own direct code-read verification, not from any lens**: the fix pass's own persisted `COMMENT ON FUNCTION` for the cursor fix described the fallback as "max uuid" when the code and the migration's own design note both use minimum uuid — corrected via a metadata-only follow-up migration (`20260730800000`, zero behavior change). `ISS-2026-053` registered in `KNOWN_ISSUES.md` (a disclosed, worked-around C-01 gap in the reused, out-of-scope `app.enqueue_job`/PLT-132 primitive) — the fix agent's own report claimed this registration but the entry did not exist; added by the orchestrating session.

> **Prior checkpoint (2026-08-07, branch `claude/cargogrid-execution-rules-o139y9`) — the execution cadence changed. Read this before starting Prompt 257 or any later prompt.**
>
> **From Prompt 257, the adversarial review round runs once per batch of at most five prompts, not once per prompt.** Ratified as `docs/adr/ADR-0021-batched-review-and-fix-execution-cadence.md`; the protocol you actually follow is `docs/standards/BUILD_EXECUTION_PROTOCOL.md`. Prompts 220–256 remain `VERIFIED` under the old per-prompt cadence and are not revisited. **No capability prompt was executed this checkpoint** — it is documentation only, and it does not authorize Prompt 257.
>
> **Three tiers. Two of them are still per-prompt, and they are the reason batching is safe.**
> **Tier A** — the automated gates, every prompt, blocking. **Tier B** — walk `docs/standards/RECURRING_DEFECT_TAXONOMY.md` §4 against your own diff and record it in the prompt's build log, every prompt, blocking. **Tier C** — the four-lens adversarial review, fix pass, propagation sweep, and independent full-suite re-verification, once per batch.
>
> **Tier B is the new thing, and it is the point.** More than 90 defects across Prompts 220–256 collapse into 20 recurring classes; every Phase 6 Critical/High is one of them. The classes kept recurring *while per-prompt review was running* — Prompt 256 reintroduced the exact `auth.uid()`-versus-actor-parameter defect Prompt 255 had already fixed, and Prompts 252/253/254 each independently reintroduced the idempotency-comparison class `ATW-030` had already swept across 20 functions. Per-prompt review was catching recurrence, not preventing it. The taxonomy attacks it at write time. Walk it honestly against the diff, not against what you meant to write.
>
> **Batches are planned before any code is written**, and five is a ceiling, never a quota. Six triggers cut a batch short on the spot — any Critical/High finding, a first-of-its-kind security mechanism, anything touching finance posting / the RBAC evaluator / RLS primitives / auth, a destructive migration, a Tier A failure you cannot clear in-prompt, or a dependency edge that would put a consumer in a different batch from its producer. Size is adaptive: 5 after a clean batch, ≤4 after one Critical/High, ≤3 after two or more.
>
> **`COMPLETED` now genuinely means adversarially unreviewed.** Only a batch's Tier C close moves prompts to `VERIFIED`, and a batch is all-or-nothing. Commit per prompt, push at least per batch — nothing survives the container that is not pushed. Run the `ISS-2026-002` pre-flight collision check once per batch, before its first prompt.
>
> **Never batch** the phase Integrated Verification / Hardening / Documentation / Closure prompts, Step 15 (368–389), Step 16 (390–412), or Step 17 (413–430). Those *are* review.
>
> **The prompt files are unchanged and you do not need to edit any of them.** All 430 stay valid — they say what to build and which gates apply, never how often review runs. One clause is narrowed: 166 capability prompts carry a §36 release gate ("Only the execution index may release `<NEXT>` … after this task is `VERIFIED`"), resolved as `CON-015` in `docs/ai-agent-build-prompt-package/00-control/04_CONFLICT_REGISTER.md` — **released on `COMPLETED` within a batch, unchanged across a batch boundary.** That is the whole override. Do not stretch it to allowed/forbidden paths, access rules, acceptance criteria, or Definition of Done. If you find a second prompt clause that cannot hold under this cadence, register it in the conflict register in the checkpoint that finds it — never resolve it silently in code.
>
> **Nothing else changed.** No gate is weaker, no applied migration may be edited, no Critical/High may be left open uncontained, and independent re-verification by the orchestrating session is still mandatory — a fix agent's self-report is still not evidence. Say so plainly if a batch is worse than the cadence assumed: `ADR-0021` has an explicit reversal condition, and using it is not a failure.
>
> **The honest limit:** this buys build speed, not publish-readiness. The Step 15 hardening and Step 16 release sequences still have to run in full, and 174 prompts before 2026-08-24 needs parallel implementation across independent dependency-graph branches as well as batching. Do not let a batch close imply more than it proves.
>
> **`CG-S11-PRC-008` (Prompt 257, Procurement RFQ) is dependency-clean but still NOT authorized.** Fresh, explicit operator authorization is required, exactly as before.

> **Follow-up within `CG-S10-ATW-032` (2026-08-05) — two of the seven "remaining" issues were fixable after all.**
>
> Asked whether the seven open issues genuinely could not be fixed, the honest answer was no — that report over-generalised. Re-examined one at a time, three were fixable and four are not. Two are now closed.
>
> **`ISS-2026-010` — default-deny landed.** The earlier reasoning ("deciding what the portal reads is Phase 8") was only half right. Deciding what it MAY read is Phase 8. Adding `not app.actor_holds_customer_user_layer(...)` removes access no design ever granted, which is the opposite of pre-empting that contract — it makes the default deny so Phase 8 opens exactly what it needs. All **98** SELECT policies whose entire test was tenant membership are narrowed. Verified live: staff read every row unchanged, a `customer_user` now reads zero. What remains open is Phase 8's own job, not a hole.
>
> **`ISS-2026-016` — wired.** The earlier call ("wiring the registry is a capability change") was wrong on the facts: `app.wms_receipt_lines` already carried `lot_number`, `serial_number`, `expiry_date` and the control flags, and `commit_wms_receipt_line` already held all of it. No signature change, no new column, no authority widening — `start_wms_receipt_session` already requires the `OPS:Create` the registry gates on, so nobody can be locked out.
>
> **`ISS-2026-019` is fixable too and is NOT done.** Its DB-level half (driver-mobile and third-party ingest load profiles, RLS isolation under load) is ordinary work against the existing harness; only the HTTPS/browser profiles need genuinely new infrastructure. It stays open with that split recorded, rather than being described as impossible.
>
> **Four genuinely need their own prompt**, and the reason is specific in each case rather than "it's tracked": `ISS-2026-018` must PICK one authoritative vehicle from a precedence chain (unlike the geofence evaluator, where "process every leg" was available and is what that fix did — tracking health is one row per shipment, so there is no such escape); `ISS-2026-031` requires choosing an arbitration threshold AND reworking `VERIFIED` fixtures that compress ~13 km into 60 s, and it changes which source wins; `ISS-2026-013` and `ISS-2026-015` are unbuilt features (per-domain entity-write adapters, a PWA and scheduler runtime), not defects.
>
> Two process notes worth carrying: the ISS-2026-016 wiring was generated twice against an already-patched probe and shipped a duplicated block that fired before the inventory movement — the receiving suite's own `serial_conflict` assertion caught it. And two migration headers were mangled by slicing a file on a phrase that also appears in its own prose. Both were caught by gates, not by review.


> **Latest checkpoint (2026-08-05, `CG-S10-ATW-032`, branch `claude/codebase-audit-fixes-yag1ow`) — Audit Register Verification and Repair.** Operator asked for verification and repair, then a straight answer on whether Prompt 249 is ready. **It is.**
>
> This is the verification phase `ISS-2026-034` existed to demand. `ATW-031` handed forward 60 unverified candidate findings with one instruction attached: do not fix from the register directly. Every claim was re-derived from the live post-migration catalogue and judged against the ratified design before being treated as a defect.
>
> **Of the 32 claims verified this round: 20 confirmed and repaired, 7 already fixed by a later migration, 5 correct by design.** Both halves of that ratio are the lesson — the finder phase surfaces real defects at a meaningful rate, and a clear majority of its claims do not survive contact with the live schema or the design documents. An unverified register is worth keeping and worth verifying, and is never worth fixing from directly.
>
> **Two findings were against this audit's own predecessors, and they are the ones to carry forward.** First: an idempotency guard that raises `unique_violation` inside a block whose own handler traps `unique_violation` is inert. All 55 guards added by `ATW-030`/`ATW-031` were swept structurally — block nesting parsed, not grepped, because a `raise exception` line contains the word "exception" and a naive scan mistakes it for a block's exception section. That is precisely what hid it. Second: 74 functions carried the version predicate on their UPDATE but never checked it applied, so a concurrent loser fell through with an unset composite, wrote a fabricated `success` audit row with a NULL `tenant_id`, and returned an all-NULL record the TypeScript guard does not catch. `20260730480000` named that residual in its own header; this closes it.
>
> **Closed:** `ISS-2026-032` — the classification pass it asked for found **33** actionable functions against its own ~315 estimate. Each already validated authority; the gap was that each asked "is the CLAIMED actor allowed?" and none asked "is the caller that actor?" `app.approve_rate_version` gated on `is_support_grant_authority(p_actor_auth_user_id, ...)`, so any session that knew a tenant_admin's UUID could approve a vendor rate as them. Also closed: `ISS-2026-034`.
>
> **`ISS-2026-010`'s stated premise was wrong and is corrected.** It assumed most tenant-scoped policies already fail closed for a `customer_user` actor because they lack an `org_unit_id`. They do not: **97** `app` SELECT policies test `has_active_tenant_membership` and nothing else, and a freshly granted `customer_user` provably satisfies it (`app.invite_user` writes the `tenant_user_identities` row that helper reads). The mechanical `AND`-narrowing is obvious, but deciding which of the 97 the portal legitimately needs IS the Phase 8 contract, so Step 13 inherits an exact, machine-checkable worklist rather than an estimate — not a half-made decision.
>
> **Open issues are now seven, all Medium, none a defect in shipped behaviour:** `ISS-2026-010` (Phase 8 portal boundary), `013` (import framework has no entity-write adapter), `015` (no PWA/scheduler runtime), `016` (lot/serial registry not yet wired into receiving), `018` (leg-level tracking precedence), `019` (load-test coverage), `031` (cross-source teleport guard). Each is a disclosed scope boundary owning its own prompt. **Zero Critical or High is open.**
>
> **Gates:** typecheck 0 errors; lint 0 errors / 85 pre-existing warnings; `pnpm test` 3091/3091; `pnpm run db:test` ALL PASSED across 155 migrations; all 8 runnable governance gates pass. `preflight` fails only because this container has no `.env` — an environment condition, not code, and secrets are never committed.
>
> **One repair went wrong first and is recorded rather than concealed:** the 74-function sweep initially blanket re-granted `authenticated, service_role`, which would have turned `app.transition_gps_device_status` — deliberately un-granted — into a public API. A standing db-test assertion caught it, and the grant block was removed entirely since `CREATE OR REPLACE` preserves an ACL. A blanket grant inside a mechanical sweep is exactly how an internal helper quietly becomes a public API.
>
> **Prompt 249 is ready to run.** Full detail: `docs/build-log/phase-05/ATW-032.md`.
