**[HISTORICAL — now resolved] This run was halted on a real blocker, not a normal checkpoint.**

Prior context (fully recorded in `ERROR_LEDGER.md` `ERR-2026-002`): two independent agent sessions — this repo's `agent/cargogrid-autonomous-build` branch, and a separate `claude/sleepy-ride-4vxsk6` branch (GitHub PR #10) — diverged from the same shared ancestor (`origin/main`@`27389a4`, PR #8) and both independently executed Prompts 46–51 (Step 3 closure, `docs/architecture/14_*.md`–`16_*.md`) and Phase 0 Prompts 80–82, producing materially different content for the same task IDs — most concretely, 607 vs. 401 traced requirement items in what was supposed to be one traceability baseline. A prior session detected this, halted immediately without starting Prompt 82 or any further work, and recorded three reconciliation options in `HANDOFF.md`/`ERROR_LEDGER.md`, explicitly stating that none of them was safe for an autonomous agent to select without operator authorization.

**What happened next (new this checkpoint):** before any operator recorded a decision, both pull requests were merged into `main` directly:
1. GitHub PR #10 (`claude/sleepy-ride-4vxsk6`) merged via `a8a197f`.
2. GitHub PR #11 (`agent/cargogrid-autonomous-build`, which had itself merged `main`'s new state at `b777bb2`) merged via `b7653cb`.

Because the two lineages' edits to `docs/architecture/14_*.md`, `15_*.md`, and `16_*.md` were pure insertions relative to their shared base rather than overlapping line-for-line, **git resolved both merges with no conflict markers by silently concatenating the two divergent lineages' full content into the same files.** This is not a reconciliation — it is literal duplication of contradictory content. Confirmed by direct inspection this checkpoint:

- `docs/architecture/14_REQUIREMENT_PHASE_TRACEABILITY.md` (1,465 lines): `## 1. Scope and method` appears at line 29 **and** line 760 — two complete copies of the document. The first states `**Total traced items** | **607**` (line 662); the second is framed against a 401-item total (line 1396: "Percentage of 401 total traced items").
- `docs/architecture/15_RISK_RANKED_CRITICAL_PATH.md` and `docs/architecture/16_STEP3_CLOSURE_REPORT.md`: each has two `## 1.` sections (confirmed via `grep -c '^## 1\.'` → 2 in both).
- `docs/runtime/HANDOFF.md`, `CARGOGRID_BUILD_STATUS.md`, and `TASK_LEDGER.md` showed the same stacking pattern for narrative/status content (now consolidated this checkpoint — see below).

Full evidence and root cause: `docs/runtime/ERROR_LEDGER.md` `ERR-2026-003` (new this checkpoint; `ERR-2026-002` is marked `SUPERSEDED` by it — read both).

**This session's actions this checkpoint (safe, non-destructive):**
1. Recreated the `agent/cargogrid-autonomous-build` branch from `origin/main`@`b7653cb` (the old branch's entire lineage is already fully contained in `main` via PR #11 — nothing was lost; `git log origin/agent/cargogrid-autonomous-build ^origin/main` is empty).
2. Recorded `ERR-2026-003` in `ERROR_LEDGER.md`, marked `ERR-2026-002` `SUPERSEDED`.
3. Added a 5th-occurrence entry to `KNOWN_ISSUES.md` `ISS-2026-002`, escalated its severity to Critical.
4. Rewrote `CARGOGRID_BUILD_STATUS.md` and this file as single coherent documents (they had each accumulated multiple stacked, contradictory sections from the two lineages' merges).
5. Added a `BLOCKED_DECISION` banner to `TASK_LEDGER.md` §2 flagging the seven duplicated/unreliable rows (`CG-S3-ARCH-014..016`, `CG-S5-PH0-001..003`) without deleting the historical duplicate rows themselves (they are evidence).

**What this session deliberately did NOT do:** it did not edit `docs/architecture/14_*.md`, `15_*.md`, or `16_*.md` themselves, and did not start `CG-S5-PH0-004`/Prompt 83 or any further Phase 0 capability prompt. Picking which of the two concatenated copies (607-item vs. 401-item traceability, and whatever else differs in `15_*.md`/`16_*.md`) is factually correct — or manually merging them fact-by-fact — is a substantive content judgment, not a mechanical cleanup, and the prior session's own reasoning for requiring operator authorization on exactly this kind of choice still applies.

**Safe to continue automatically: `YES` (as of this checkpoint).** The operator decision is recorded below; the reconciliation is complete and verified. The next runtime agent resumes Phase 0 execution at `CG-S5-PH0-004`.

### The exact question for the operator

`docs/architecture/14_REQUIREMENT_PHASE_TRACEABILITY.md`, `15_RISK_RANKED_CRITICAL_PATH.md`, and `16_STEP3_CLOSURE_REPORT.md` on `main` each contain two complete, contradictory drafts of the same Step 3 closeout deliverable, produced by two independent agent sessions from the same starting point. Which should become the single authoritative version of each file?

1. **Adopt lineage A** — the first copy in each file (originally produced on `agent/cargogrid-autonomous-build`; `14_*.md` lines 1–759, 607 traced items). Discard the second copy. Lowest-effort; discards lineage B's independent analysis (which additionally completed Prompt 82/`CG-S5-PH0-003`, not present in lineage A).
2. **Adopt lineage B** — the second copy in each file (originally produced on `claude/sleepy-ride-4vxsk6`/PR #10; `14_*.md` lines 760–1465, 401 traced items, plus its own completed Prompt 82). Discard the first copy.
3. **Reconcile manually** — compare both copies of all three files section-by-section (precedent: `CG-S2-DISC-001-R1`, `ERROR_LEDGER.md` `ERR-2026-001`), produce one merged authoritative version per file, and document which specific facts/counts from each lineage were kept and why. Highest effort, most likely to preserve the most accurate combined analysis; if chosen, should probably also re-run Prompt 82 (Requirement Traceability Baseline adoption) fresh against the reconciled `14_*.md` rather than trusting either lineage's already-completed Prompt 82 output, since both were built on top of one specific copy.

**DECISION RECORDED (2026-07-15, operator-authorized via runtime session):** **Option 1 — adopt Lineage A** as the single authoritative version of all three files. Rationale: a fresh evidence-based re-analysis found Lineage A's 607-item total internally self-consistent (by-source and by-state tables both cross-foot to 607), while Lineage B's 401-item total was internally *inconsistent* (its own breakdown formula summed to 469 and its by-state row to 385 — an arithmetic defect), and Lineage B's `16_*.md` copy carried a raw git-diff artifact as a heading. Both lineages' per-catalogue counts agree where they overlap, so adopting A discards no correct mapping. Full analysis and the executed recovery steps are in `ERROR_LEDGER.md` `ERR-2026-003` (now `RECOVERED`). Prompt 82 was re-verified against the 607 baseline (`docs/build-log/phase-00/PH0-82.md`); Phase 0 build logs were standardized on the prompt-package-prescribed singular path and the plural Lineage B duplicates removed. **This blocker is resolved — the sections below are retained as the historical record of the incident.**

fails (`Only lon/lat coordinate systems are supported in geography`), making SRID 4326 (WGS84) impossible to drift from — no separate SRID CHECK constraint is needed. **A real, concrete defect class found by this checkpoint's own testing, not hypothesized**: PostGIS's own `geometry -> geography` cast does **not** reject an out-of-range latitude/longitude — it silently *coerces* (clamps) the value into range (`ST_MakePoint(0, 95)::geography` succeeds with a `NOTICE`, proven directly) — exactly the "silent axis swap" Prompt 134 §19/§23 forbid. `app.geojson_point_to_geography()` explicitly 500,000-meter (500km) bounded-radius query cap enforced by `app.bounded_st_dwithin()`, the one governed choke point for any future proximity query, proven with a real `EXPLAIN`-plan check that a GiST index on a `geography` column is actually used, not a sequential scan, plus a tenant-scoped + bounded-radius query on the representative example proving cross-tenant isolation and correct near/far distinction (Jakarta-Bogor ~40km within a 100km search, Jakarta-Singapore ~880km outside it). **Two real, disclosed environment gaps were found and fixed before any migration could be tested, not defects in the migration itself**: this sandbox's Postgres 16 install had no PostGIS package at all (`pg_available_extensions` returned zero `postgis*` rows) — fixed via `apt-get install postgresql-16-postgis-3` (resolves to 3.4.2), confirmed with a direct `CREATE EXTENSION`/`PostGIS_Version()` smoke test in a scratch database before writing the real migration; CI's `db` job used a plain `postgres:17` service image with no PostGIS — fixed by switching to `postgis/postgis:17-3.4` (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`), the same Postgres major version this repository already pins (`ADR-0004`) with PostGIS added, not a second unpinned version axis. `server/contracts/spatial/spatial.ts` (new) mirrors the governed GeoJSON/radius conventions client-side (validation-only, - **Phase 1 Prompt 133** (`CG-S6-PLT-030`): Feature Flags — authorized by a fresh, separate, unscoped user "lanjut" in a new session, exactly the task `PLT-132`'s own handoff named as the sole next-eligible task. **A flag's target/version storage is not a new table family — it reuses `PLT-121`'s Configuration Engine directly** (`config_object`/`config_version`/`config_item`), the same "shared mechanism, not a fork" pattern `PLT-122`/`124` already established; `app.register_feature_flag()` mints its own dedicated `'feature:<flag_key>'` config_type per flag (Supreme-only, idempotent), the same reason `PLT-124`'s status sets each mint their own. **Feature flags support only global/tenant scope, a deliberate, structurally-enforced narrowing** of the Configuration Engine's full 6-level precedence — Tech Arch's own flag dimensions never included an org-hierarchy company/branch/role/user precedence. **The kill switch and environment allowlist are database-enforced global-only, non-overridable-at-tenant-scope dimensions** — `app.set_feature_flag_items()` structurally rejects any attempt to set `kill_switch`/`environments` at tenant scope, so no tenant `tenant_state='allow'` override can ever resurrect a platform-wide kill; this is this checkpoint's own considered resolution of a real tension the Phase 0 evaluator's single-object precedence did not have to face, since Platform Core's own config-engine precedence model is most-specific-wins by default. Proven end-to-end in `scripts/db-tests/feature-flags.sql`: a tenant `allow` override published *after* a global kill still evaluates to `kill_switch`, not `tenant_override_allow`. **`app.evaluate_feature_flag()`** (`SECURITY DEFINER`, the fixed 10-reason precedence order: unknown flag → entitlement → unconfigured → kill_switch → environment_gate → tenant deny/allow → cohort_mismatch → rollout_bucket → default) is entitlement-aware via `PLT-106`'s read-only `app.evaluate_entitlement()` — an unentitled tenant is denied `module_not_entitled` regardless of rollout percentage — but has **no code path that can grant any permission/module entitlement/RLS bypass**, structurally, not by convention, the same discipline `scripts/feature-flags/flags.ts`'s own Phase 0 header established, carried forward rather than weakened. Bucketing is SQL-native (`md5`-based, deterministic `[0,99]`), a disclosed, non-bit-identical divergence from the Phase 0 module's `sha256`-based `bucketFor()` — Prompt 133 §14 names this checkpoint's own evaluator as the Platform Core authority; the Phase 0 module is untouched and remains available for its own callers. `app.kill_feature_flag()` is the Supreme-only panic-button convenience path (preserves the other currently-published dimensions while forcing `kill_switch=true`, reversible via `PLT-121`'s own unmodified `app.rollback_config_version()`); `app.debug_feature_flag()` is the privileged, self-audited explain path, authority-gated identically to `app.check_config_object_authority`'s own split. `FeatureFlagCache` (`server/queries/feature-flags.ts`, keyed by flag/tenant/environment/cohort-set) is explicitly `invalidate()`d by every mutation — Prompt 133 §17's "prompt invalidation," proven directly. **One real defect found and fixed in this checkpoint's own db-test/migration authoring, not a design flaw**: a raw `jsonb` array passed to `PLT-116`'s `capture_audit_event()` broke `app.redact_audit_payload()`'s internal `jsonb_each()` call (which requires a JSON object, not an array) — fixed by wrapping in `jsonb_build_object('items', ...)`, the same shape every other Configuration-Engine-derived capability's own generic audit call already uses. - **Phase 1 Prompt 132** (`CG-S6-PLT-029`): Background Job Framework — authorized by a sixth, separate, unscoped user "lanjut," sent mid-checkpoint while `PLT-131`'s own paperwork was still being finished and confirmed to carry forward to this task once `PLT-131` closed. **Widens `PLT-131`'s `app.jobs`, does not create it** — exactly as that checkpoint's own migration header disclosed would happen here: `job_type` widened from `('import','export')` to a ten-code allowlist. The eight new codes are the real, sourced job-type use-case list `02_CANONICAL_DATA_FLOW_MAP.md` §5.3 names verbatim (report generation, notification batch, webhook retry, document/PDF generation, dashboard refresh, loyalty expiration, recurring billing, integration sync) -- **no business logic for any of them is implemented here**, only the generic queue mechanics (enqueue/claim/heartbeat/complete/fail/DLQ/requeue) a future domain-specific worker would sit on top of, matching Prompt 132 §12's own "no domain batch implementation" prohibition. **`app.jobs.locked_by`/`locked_until` are wired to a real atomic claim function for the first time** (`app.claim_next_job()`) -- exactly as `PLT-131`'s migration header disclosed was deferred to this task. `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` is PostgreSQL's own standard, documented-since-9.5 mechanism for "no two workers claim the same row" -- genuinely provable without a live multi-process worker fleet, since `SKIP LOCKED`'s row-level locking semantics are a documented PostgreSQL guarantee, not something this migration invents; a stale lease (an `in_progress` row whose `locked_until` has passed, meaning the worker that held it crashed) is structurally eligible for reclaim by the same claim query -- the real crash-recovery mechanic proven directly in this checkpoint's own db-test. **`ADR-0013` newly ratifies a freshly-minted `ADR-CAND-ARCH-028`** (this checkpoint's own research confirmed no existing candidate covers job-queue backoff/lease/DLQ numeric thresholds anywhere) -- equal-jitter exponential backoff (base 30s, 2x multiplier, capped at 3600s, the well-known AWS "equal jitter" strategy) and a 300s default worker lease, the same "reasoned default absent blueprint-mandated numbers" class `ADR-0002`/`ADR-0004`/`ADR-0011`/`ADR-0012` already used. **`app.event_logs` (the outbox-pattern table) is built here, closing a three-document-deep deferral chain** traced back to `PLT-116`'s own migration header ("`JOB`/`API-WH` are `PLT-129..132`, far downstream") and re-confirmed by `PLT-130`'s build log -- `app.append_event_log()` is the write path, `app.dispatch_event_as_job()` is the real, idempotent outbox-drain mechanic (re-dispatching an already-dispatched event returns the same linked job, never double-enqueues), the live scheduler that would call it periodically disclosed `NOT_RUN` (no cron/poll infrastructure exists anywhere in this repository yet). Since an event-dispatched job has no human requester, `app.jobs.requested_by_auth_user_id`'s `NOT NULL` constraint is dropped -- RLS's own `requested_by_auth_user_id = auth.uid()` branch simply never matches a null value, so a system-dispatched job is visible only to Supreme Admin/support authority, proven directly. **`app.jobs`' existing `authenticated`-scoped RLS policy (`PLT-131`) is deliberately left unchanged**, not tightened to the `service_role_only` family `06_RLS_RBAC_WORKSTREAM.md` names for it -- this checkpoint's own research found that conflict and disclosed it as an intentional continuation of `PLT-131`'s already-shipped, already-tested precedent, not an oversight. **`PLT-131`'s own `cancel_import_export_job`/`acknowledge_job_cancellation`/`record_job_failure`/`requeue_dead_letter_job` are reused directly for the generic job lifecycle, not forked** -- despite their import/export-flavored names, none of their bodies ever inspected `job_type`; three are extended via `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` (lease release, `next_attempt_at` scheduling/clearing) with identical signatures and identical exception message prefixes preserved verbatim, proven non-breaking by re-running `PLT-131`'s own unmodified db-test suite against this checkpoint's migration before writing any new test -- it still passes in full. **One real defect found and fixed in this checkpoint's own db-test authoring, not the migration**: a scenario-group cross-contamination -- an earlier scenario group's deliberately-left-pending job (created only to prove idempotency) was claimed by a later, unrelated scenario group's `claim_next_job()` call instead of that group's own just-enqueued job, since `claim_next_job`'s own priority ordering (working exactly as designed) preferred the older, higher-priority leftover row. Fixed by giving the later group's job a deterministically-higher priority and adding an explicit assertion the claimed job matches the one just enqueued. `node:test` 866/866, `db:test` 350 total scenario groups across 29 migrations, 10/11 gates green -- `test:e2e` `NOT_RUN` in this sandbox (same disclosed Playwright browser-binary revision skew as every checkpoint since `PLT-117`). **This checkpoint was authorized by a single unscoped "lanjut," not a range -- the next runtime agent must stop and obtain fresh explicit user authorization before proceeding to `CG-S6-PLT-030` (Prompt 133, Feature Flags) or beyond.** Full detail: `docs/build-log/phase-01/PLT-132.md`.

An import/export schema is a per-instance `import_export:<code>` config registry, `PLT-121`'s registry pattern reused directly, not forked. Row validation (`app.validate_staging_row()`) is structural-only (required-ness + per-`data_type` shape) — never a domain write, since no business-domain table exists yet anywhere in Phase 1 Platform Core; `app.commit_import_job()`'s "commit" means "finalize validation," not "write to a real table." Every prior checkpoint's "`pnpm run test`: PASS" was accurate for a smaller test set than believed. Fixed by quoting the globs in `package.json` so Node's own glob engine resolves `**` recursively. The database `CHECK` constraint was never at risk; this was a missing TypeScript-layer defense-in-depth guarantee only. pagination (opaque base64url cursors encoding keyset-ordering values, never a raw offset, capped at 100 items per page — the concrete unbounded-payload guard), idempotency-key/correlation-id contracts access-evaluation pipeline `06_RLS_RBAC_WORKSTREAM.md` §3 fixes (1 entitlement → 2 tenant membership → 3 RBAC action → 4 scope [no-op, disclosed, no scope model exists yet] → 5 field/record-level responsibility, not this pipeline's]), built **entirely** from already-tested `PLT-106`'s `evaluateEntitlement()`, `PLT-108`'s `resolveAccessContext()`, `PLT-112`'s `assertPermission()` (the exact - **Phase 1 Prompt 129** (`CG-S6-PLT-026`): API Key and Webhook Primitives — authorized by a third, separate, unscoped user "lanjut" following the `PLT-127`/`PLT-128` checkpoints' own single-task "lanjut" grants. Tenant-scoped hashed API key lifecycle (create-once-display, overlap-window rotation, revoke, real scope-narrowing enforcement) plus webhook endpoint/signed-delivery primitives (HMAC-SHA256 signing, timestamp-tolerance replay protection, retry/backoff/DLQ, consecutive-failure auto-disable). **API key scopes can only narrow what the actor's own role already holds, never widen it** — a scope string (`<resource_module_code>:<action>`, `app.permissions`'s own natural key) is validated at creation time against the actor's own currently-granted RBAC permissions via `app.evaluate_permission()` (`PLT-112`, composed directly, not re-implemented), proven with a real role/permission/assignment chain in the db-test, not merely asserted. **Neither a raw API key nor a raw webhook secret is ever stored in a re-requestable form** — a key persists only as a one-way sha256 digest (`key_hash`); a webhook secret is stored directly (HMAC verification is symmetric, so the platform's own delivery worker genuinely needs the raw value to sign outgoing requests, a real difference from the key case) but the table carries zero `authenticated` grant at all — both return exactly once from their create/rotate RPC. **`ADR-0011` newly ratified** (`docs/adr/ADR-0011-webhook-signature-and-auto-disable-thresholds.md`), resolving `ADR-CAND-ARCH-018`'s signature/timestamp/auto-disable sub-questions with the candidate's own recommended defaults — HMAC-SHA256 signing, a 5-minute timestamp tolerance, a 10-consecutive-failure auto-disable threshold — all real, tested, and provable without any live HTTP call, since HMAC computation is pure cryptographic computation reusing `pgcrypto`'s `hmac()`/`digest()`/`gen_random_bytes()` (already unconditionally enabled since `PLT-105`, zero new extension dependency). A bounded, disclosed-partial SSRF check (`app.validate_webhook_url()`) rejects a literal `localhost`/loopback/private/link-local IPv4/IPv6 host at registration time — honestly disclosed as unable to defend against DNS-rebinding-based SSRF, which requires live delivery-time DNS resolution belonging to the not-yet-built delivery worker. **This entire capability is deliberately server-mediated only**, the design `PLT-128` established — every mutating function is `service_role`-only, so none of them needs `SECURITY DEFINER` except the two authenticated-facing listing functions (`list_api_keys_for_tenant`/`list_webhook_endpoints_for_tenant`), which need it to read `service_role`-only tables while excluding `key_hash`/`secret_value` from their result shape entirely. Webhook delivery itself is never real (disclosed `NOT_RUN`, no live HTTP client anywhere in this repository) — `app.record_webhook_delivery_attempt()` is the real, tested, bounded adapter interface a future delivery worker would call, applying the `'success'`/`'failure'` audit-result mapping proactively (`PLT-127`'s own lesson). **One real bug found and fixed via an actual failing test, not self-review**: `app.rotate_api_key()`/`app.rotate_webhook_secret()` both declared `returns table (id uuid, ...)`, which auto-declares `id` as a PL/pgSQL variable in that function's own scope — their internal bare `where id = p_key_id`-shaped lookups then raised "column reference \"id\" is ambiguous" the moment the db-test actually exercised rotation, the exact `RETURNS TABLE` column-shadowing bug class `PLT-117` found in `app.evaluate_tenant_brand()` — recognized immediately from that precedent and fixed the same way, by table-qualifying every affected bare `id` reference. `node:test` 601/601, `db:test` 317 total scenario groups across 26 migrations, 10/11 gates green — `test:e2e` `NOT_RUN` in this sandbox (same disclosed Playwright browser-binary revision skew as every checkpoint since `PLT-117`; a stray gitignored `playwright-report/` artifact from an earlier `test:e2e` attempt this checkpoint also tripped a transient, unrelated `lint` failure on a bundled trace-viewer file, deleted since it was never tracked or part of this diff). **This checkpoint was authorized by a single unscoped "lanjut," not a range — the next runtime agent must stop and obtain fresh explicit user authorization before proceeding to `CG-S6-PLT-027` (Prompt 130, REST/GraphQL Platform API Foundation) or beyond.** Full detail: `docs/build-log/phase-01/PLT-129.md`.
- **Phase 1 Prompt 128** (`CG-S6-PLT-025`): Document and File Engine — authorized by a second, separate, unscoped user "lanjut" following `PLT-127`'s own single-task "lanjut" checkpoint. A document *type* reuses `PLT-121`'s Configuration Engine directly, minting a dedicated `document:<code>` config_type per instance via `app.register_config_type()` — the same per-instance registry pattern established at `PLT-124`/`125`/`126`/`127` (unlike those four, no generic `'document'` config_type was ever seeded in the first place, so there is no unused placeholder to disclose here). **No real storage or malware-scan provider exists anywhere in this repository** — `app.files.storage_path` is a real, unguessable, server-generated object key (`tenant_id` + `document_type_code` + a fresh random uuid, deliberately never derived from the client-supplied filename, the structural reason path traversal via that vector is impossible); `app.record_file_scan_result()` is a real, tested, bounded adapter interface a future scan-provider webhook/job would call, never invoked against a fabricated scanner itself. **This entire capability is deliberately server-mediated only** — every mutating function is `service_role`-only, never `authenticated`, since real signed-URL issuance inherently requires storage credentials only server code holds; consequently none of its functions needs `SECURITY DEFINER` (`service_role` already holds direct table grants on everything they touch). Reads are direct-table RLS (the "shared business data" precedent, not `PLT-127`'s "personal inbox" precedent). `app.authorize_file_access()` composes two independent gates — the malware-scan gate (an `infected` result blocks even the uploader; a not-yet-`clean` result blocks everyone but the uploader) and the record/sensitivity access gate (`app.can_access_record()` plus a stricter check for `restricted`/`credential` classification) — and fails safely: a zero-standing actor is refused outright with no log row, while a merely-denied tenant member gets a real, logged denial in the new `app.file_access_logs` table. RLS on `app.files` deliberately does **not** reference `malware_scan_status` at all, since metadata/status visibility ("pending scan is visible with safe status") is a separate concern from content-delivery authorization, composing `app.can_access_record()` plus a classification gate plus a deleted-row visibility restriction instead. **`classification` is wired to `scripts/data-classification/registry.ts`'s `SENSITIVITY_LEVELS` scale** via a literal `CHECK`-constraint match (not a static `ClassificationEntry`, since a file's classification is a dynamic per-row value, not a fixed schema-field sensitivity) — resolving that standard's own §8-named gap ("files.classification column values wired to this registry — No files table exists yet"), updated in the same checkpoint. Legal hold genuinely blocks deletion until released (support/Supreme-authority-only to set); versioning always inserts a new row — never overwrites — flipping the prior row to `superseded` *before* inserting the new one so a partial unique index (`version_group_id) where is_latest_version`) is never transiently violated, keeping exactly one current version per lineage. **An additive grant was needed**: `app.is_support_grant_authority()` (`PLT-115`, originally `service_role`-only) is granted to `authenticated` here for the first time, so the new RLS policy on `app.files` can evaluate it as the calling role — verified safe, since the function's own internal lookup always targets the caller's own `principal_memberships` row, which passes that table's existing RLS policy regardless of branch. **No real defect was found or fixed during this checkpoint** — the full `scripts/db-tests/document-file.sql` suite (14 scenario groups) and every TypeScript gate passed on the first complete run, honestly disclosed as a first for this session's PLT-12x sequence (every prior engine checkpoint found and fixed at least one real bug via a failing test) rather than implying extra rigor was applied — the design deliberately reused more already-proven primitives and lessons (`can_access_record`, `capture_audit_event`, the config engine, `PLT-127`'s own `SECURITY DEFINER`/`audit_logs.result` findings) applied proactively before the first test run than any prior checkpoint. `node:test` 572/572, `db:test` 300 total scenario groups across 25 migrations, 10/11 gates green — `test:e2e` `NOT_RUN` in this sandbox (same disclosed Playwright browser-binary revision skew as every checkpoint since `PLT-117`). **This checkpoint was authorized by a single unscoped "lanjut," not a range — the next runtime agent must stop and obtain fresh explicit user authorization before proceeding to `CG-S6-PLT-026` (Prompt 129, API Key and Webhook Primitives) or beyond.** Full detail: `docs/build-log/phase-01/PLT-128.md`.
- **Phase 1 Prompt 127** (`CG-S6-PLT-024`): Notification Engine — authorized by a single unscoped user "lanjut" (one task, not a range) following the close of the "lanjut sd prompt 126" range. A notification *template* reuses `PLT-121`'s Configuration Engine directly, minting a dedicated `notification:<code>` config_type per instance via `app.register_config_type()` — the same per-instance registry pattern `PLT-124`'s Status Engine established (the single shared seeded `'notification'` config_type remains an unused template placeholder, explicitly disclosed as such). **No real external provider send exists anywhere in this repository** — `in_app` delivery is real and immediate (the row's existence is the delivery); `email`/future channels are real only through `status='queued'`; `app.record_notification_delivery_attempt()` is a real, tested, bounded adapter interface a future provider integration would call, never a fabricated send. Template escaping is real and layered: static template text is protected at definition time by the inherited `app.validate_config_value()` `<>` rejection (already enforced on every `config_items` row since `PLT-121`); dynamic context/event data is protected at render time by a new explicit `<>` rejection inside `app.render_notification_template()`, correctly targeting the real injection vector (untrusted context data, not tenant-authored template text). Link safety is a hybrid real check — a `scheme://`-shaped value must be `https://`, plus a narrow known-dangerous-scheme blocklist (`javascript:`/`data:`/`vbscript:`) for schemes with no `//`, deliberately narrow to avoid false-positiving on ordinary colon-containing text. Recipient authorization fails safely: `app.queue_notification()` requires `has_active_tenant_membership(tenant, recipient)` for the *recipient* independent of the triggering actor's own authority, refusing outright for a wrong-tenant/deactivated recipient. Preference-aware fallback is real: a disabled requested channel silently redirects to `in_app` if allowed-and-not-itself-disabled, otherwise the attempt is still recorded (never silently dropped) as `status='skipped'`. RLS is deliberately tighter than every other engine this session (self-only `recipient_auth_user_id = auth.uid()`/`auth_user_id = auth.uid()` plus Supreme, not "any active tenant member," since this is a personal inbox, not shared business data). **Two real bugs found via actual failing `pnpm run db:test` runs (not self-review), both fixed and re-verified**: (1) the original link-safety check only matched `scheme://`-shaped patterns, so a `javascript:alert(1)` context value slipped through unrejected since `javascript:` URIs carry no `//` — fixed by adding the explicit dangerous-scheme blocklist; (2) `app.record_notification_delivery_attempt()` originally passed its own `p_status` literal (`'failed'`) directly as `capture_audit_event()`'s result argument, but `app.audit_logs.result`'s `CHECK` constraint only allows `('success','failure')` — fixed with a `case` mapping `'failed'` status to audit result `'failure'`. `node:test` 550/550, `db:test` 286 total scenario groups across 24 migrations, 10/11 gates green — `test:e2e` `NOT_RUN` in this sandbox (same disclosed Playwright browser-binary revision skew as every checkpoint since `PLT-117`). **This checkpoint was authorized by a single unscoped "lanjut," not a range — the next runtime agent must stop and obtain fresh explicit user authorization before proceeding to `CG-S6-PLT-025` (Prompt 128, Document/File Engine) or beyond.** Full detail: `docs/build-log/phase-01/PLT-127.md`.
- **Phase 1 Prompt 126** (`CG-S6-PLT-023`): Form and Custom-Field Builder — the fifth and final task in the user's "lanjut sd prompt 126" range, closing it in full. A form *definition* is not a new table family either: `app.register_form()` mints a dedicated `form:<code>` config_type per form via `PLT-121`'s own `app.register_config_type()`, the identical registry composition `PLT-124`'s Status Engine established one checkpoint before Workflow/Approval/Numbering also adopted it, for the identical structural reason (the single shared seeded `'form'` type can host at most one tenant-scoped object). **EAV query explosion is structurally avoided** (Prompt 126 §13, explicitly named as a forbidden pattern) — `app.custom_field_values` holds exactly one row per `(tenant, entity_type, entity_id)`, every field's value packed into a single `values jsonb` column, proven directly to stay at exactly one row even after an update (never a fan-out of one row per field, the classic EAV anti-pattern). Field types (`text`/`textarea`/`number`/`boolean`/`date`/`select`/`multiselect`/`email`) and validators (`min_length`/`max_length`/`min`/`max`/`pattern`) are both real, bounded allowlists — `file` is deliberately excluded, disclosed rather than fabricated, since no file-storage plumbing exists anywhere in this repository yet. A field's visibility `condition` is a bounded, structural `{field_code, operator, value}` comparison, never an expression language, and may only reference a field declared *earlier* in the same array — making a condition cycle structurally impossible by construction, a deliberately different (and simpler) resolution than `PLT-122`'s own bounded-walk reachability check, chosen because forms have no legitimate need for cyclic conditions the way workflows do for revision loops. `app.custom_field_values` reuses `PLT-121`'s own `app.validate_config_value()` directly as its `values` column `CHECK` constraint — composing the existing recursive injection-safety gate rather than reimplementing it. **Sensitive custom-field values get a real, narrower read gate** (§18) — a values row with no sensitive field is readable by any active tenant member; a row with at least one `sensitive: true` field additionally requires being the original submitter or holding the same definition-admin-grade authority that manages the form itself, proven directly against a genuinely denied third, unrelated regular `org_user` (not the submitter, not an admin). `app.set_custom_field_values()` runs the real submission-time validation gate *before* ever writing, proven that an invalid submission leaves zero rows behind. One test-authoring mistake (calling `app.invite_user()` before the corresponding `auth.users` fixture row existed for a newly-introduced third test identity) was caught via the real foreign-key violation it correctly produced, and fixed before the suite was declared green. `node:test` 533/533, `db:test` 277 total scenario groups, 10/11 gates green — `test:e2e` `NOT_RUN` in this sandbox (same disclosed Playwright browser-binary revision skew as `PLT-117..125`). **This closes the user's "lanjut sd prompt 126" range in full (Prompts 122–126, all five now `VERIFIED`).** Per this build's own standing discipline (identical to the "sd prompt 120" range's close before `PLT-121`), **the next runtime agent/session must stop and obtain fresh explicit user authorization before proceeding to `CG-S6-PLT-024` (Prompt 127, Notification Engine) or beyond** — its dependencies are all `VERIFIED` and it is dependency-`READY`, but authorization, not dependency-readiness, is the active gate now. Full detail: `docs/build-log/phase-01/PLT-126.md`.
- **Phase 1 Prompt 125** (`CG-S6-PLT-022`): Numbering Engine — configurable, collision-safe numbering with versioned formats/scopes/reservations/audit, layered on `PLT-121`'s own config engine (a definition is a `config_type_code='numbering'` config_version, reused directly, not a new table family). `app.validate_numbering_definition()` requires exactly one `{SEQ}` token over a real bounded token allowlist (`{YYYY}`/`{YY}`/`{MM}`/`{DD}`/`{SEQ}`/`{SCOPE_CODE}`) — the same "no arbitrary executable code" guardrail every prior engine checkpoint has honored. `app.allocate_numbering_seq()` is a single atomic `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT ... DO UPDATE ... RETURNING` statement, Postgres's own standard safe counter pattern. **`app.numbering_allocations`' own `unique(tenant_id, formatted_number)` constraint is the real, final collision guarantee — and it actually caught a genuine collision during this checkpoint's own test authoring**: the first draft of the representative `INV-{YYYY}-{SEQ}` example format omitted `{SCOPE_CODE}`, so when the test allocated under two different `scope_key` values (both independently and correctly reaching `seq=1`), both rendered the identical literal string and the real unique constraint rejected the second insert — not a database bug, the collision-safety guarantee working exactly as designed against test data that hadn't actually differentiated its scope keys in the rendered output. Fixed by correcting the representative format to `INV-{SCOPE_CODE}-{YYYY}-{SEQ}` (a more realistic multi-branch invoice format), disclosed directly in the build log as a real, useful proof point rather than quietly reworked. Allocated numbers are never silently recycled — `app.release_number_reservation()`/`app.void_number_allocation()` are both terminal, audited, and proven to never free their consumed seq for reuse (the next real allocation is always `seq+1`, verified directly). `app.bootstrap_numbering_counter()` seeds a counter's last-used value directly and structurally refuses to ever lower an existing counter, so legacy import never renumbers historical records. `node:test` 521/521, `db:test` 268 total scenario groups, 10/11 gates green — `test:e2e` `NOT_RUN` in this sandbox (same disclosed Playwright browser-binary revision skew as `PLT-117..124`). **This was the fourth of five tasks the user's "lanjut sd prompt 126" range authorizes; `CG-S6-PLT-023` (Prompt 126, Form and Custom-Field Builder) is the final one — once it completes, the next runtime agent must stop and obtain fresh explicit user authorization before proceeding to Prompt 127 or beyond.** Full detail: `docs/build-log/phase-01/PLT-125.md`.
- **Phase 1 Prompt 124** (`CG-S6-PLT-021`): Status Engine — a permanent canonical status registry (`app.status_sets`/`app.canonical_statuses`) with versioned tenant presentation layered on `PLT-121`'s own config engine. "Workflow/authorization controls transitions; status engine describes/resolves" (Prompt 124 §24) — this capability never decides whether a transition is legal, only what a status permanently *means* and how it is tenant-labeled. Canonical statuses are registered once (Supreme-only, idempotent) and never mutated in place — only presentation is ever versioned. Since `config_objects_scope_shape_check` forces `scope_id` null at `scope_level='tenant'`, the single shared, `PLT-121`-seeded generic `'status'` config_type could host at most one tenant-scoped object ever — insufficient for multiple independently-versioned status sets (Quotation, Shipment, Invoice, ...). `app.register_status_set()` resolves this by minting a dedicated `status:<code>` config_type per set via `PLT-121`'s own `app.register_config_type()`, the same "registry, not enum" composition discipline `PLT-120`/`122` already established, rather than forking a parallel mechanism — the originally-seeded generic `'status'` type stays exactly the unused template placeholder `PLT-121` disclosed it to be. **Accessible non-color cues are structurally required, not optional** (Prompt 124 §15) — `app.validate_status_presentation()` rejects a color-only status label at publish time, proven directly. `app.resolve_status_presentation()` composes `PLT-121`'s own 6-level precedence resolver, falling back to a structural, code-derived label and neutral icon (never a fabricated tenant-specific color) when nothing has published yet — proven to "fall back safely" (§22) rather than error. `app.status_legacy_mappings` carries a real `unique(status_set_code, legacy_value)` "no silent collision" guarantee (§25), proven against a genuine remap attempt. **Zero real domain status sets are seeded in this migration** — `docs/architecture/07_CONFIGURATION_ENGINE_WORKSTREAM.md` §7.2's real, sourced 24 status transitions across 6 domain entities (Lead/Quotation/Shipment/Invoice/Vendor/Ticket) are all explicitly Phase 2+ Module Adoption Map scope, verbatim: "Bootstrap all 4 [Workflow/Status/Numbering/Form] as empty templates." The one safe representative example this checkpoint uses is the real, sourced Quotation shape (`Draft → Under Approval → Approved → Sent → Accepted|Expired`) — built and proven entirely inside `scripts/db-tests/status.sql`, no such row exists anywhere in the migration itself. One test-ordering mistake (the same "stale config_items from a prior failure-mode test" class already caught in `PLT-122`/`123`'s own authoring) found and fixed while writing the db-test file, before the suite was declared green. `node:test` 504/504, `db:test` 258 total scenario groups, 10/11 gates green — `test:e2e` `NOT_RUN` in this sandbox (same disclosed Playwright browser-binary revision skew as `PLT-117..123`). **The user's "lanjut sd prompt 126" range remains open — this was the third of five tasks it authorizes (Prompts 122–126); the next runtime agent should continue sequentially into `CG-S6-PLT-022` without requesting fresh authorization.** Full detail: `docs/build-log/phase-01/PLT-124.md`.
- **Phase 1 Prompt 123** (`CG-S6-PLT-020`): Approval Engine — an approval *definition* is not a new table family either, it reuses `PLT-121`'s own `config_type_code='approval'` config_object/config_version/config_items directly, the same discipline `PLT-122`'s workflow definitions established one checkpoint earlier. This migration adds `app.validate_approval_definition()` (publish-time structural gate — 9 distinct failure modes proven individually: invalid pattern, missing steps, duplicated step_order, unknown approver_type, missing approver ref, unknown role, unknown user, invalid required_approvals, invalid threshold), and the runtime `app.approval_requests`/`app.approval_request_steps`/`app.approval_decisions`/`app.approval_delegations` tables with request/decide/cancel/escalate/delegate lifecycle functions. **All three routing patterns are proven as genuinely distinct algorithms, not the same code under three names**: `sequential` (steps open one at a time, a rejection anywhere fails the whole request immediately), `parallel` (every step opens at once, the request approves only once every step independently reaches its own required_approvals count), `threshold` (every step opens at once, but the request approves the instant N of the total step count individually reach approved, and every still-open step is then marked skipped). Approver resolution reuses `PLT-112`'s own disclosed role-assignment-join-to-published-version convention verbatim (a stale assignment pointing at a since-superseded role version fails closed, never silently grants approval authority). Separation-of-duties self-approval denial is proven against a requester who is *also* a genuinely eligible approver, so the denial demonstrably comes from the self-approval gate, not incidental ineligibility. Delegation is real and bounded (`CHECK`-enforced <=90 days, a constructed, disclosed default matching `PLT-115`'s support-grant expiry-cap posture) — proven to fail safely the instant a delegation is revoked (Prompt 123 §23's "expired delegation... fails safely"), and deliberately not followed transitively across chains (a bounded scope choice that also structurally rules out a delegation-cycle infinite loop). `app.request_approval()` proactively refuses to create a request against any step with zero currently-eligible approvers, rather than creating an undecidable request that would sit pending forever. `app.escalate_approval_step()` is the real, audited manual SLA-escalation action (gated by definition-admin-grade authority, not the looser instance-level requester/approver authority) — the automatic timer trigger that would invoke it is disclosed `NOT_RUN` (no scheduler/cron infrastructure exists anywhere in this repository yet). The one safe synthetic example (a 2-step sequential manager-then-finance approval, plus parallel/threshold variants) is built and proven entirely inside `scripts/db-tests/approval.sql` — no example row seeded in the migration. **Three real mistakes caught and fixed before any gate ran**: an initial 5-argument call to `app.check_config_object_authority()` (the real function takes 3); a `jsonb::boolean` direct cast (needed `#>> '{}'` first, the same text-extraction pattern used elsewhere); and a delegation-eligibility check that initially called itself recursively with no depth bound (a genuine infinite-loop risk on a 2-identity delegation cycle) — fixed by deliberately scoping delegation to one hop only. `node:test` 487/487, `db:test` 249 total scenario groups, 10/11 gates green — `test:e2e` `NOT_RUN` in this sandbox (same disclosed Playwright browser-binary revision skew as `PLT-117..122`). **The user's "lanjut sd prompt 126" range remains open — this was the second of five tasks it authorizes (Prompts 122–126); the next runtime agent should continue sequentially into `CG-S6-PLT-021` without requesting fresh authorization.** Full detail: `docs/build-log/phase-01/PLT-123.md`.
- **Phase 1 Prompt 122** (`CG-S6-PLT-019`): Workflow Engine — a workflow *definition* is not a new table family, it is `PLT-121`'s own `config_type_code='workflow'` config_object/config_version/config_items reused directly (the same "shared mechanism, not a fork" discipline `PLT-120`/`121` established). This migration adds `app.workflow_hooks` (a real, bounded guard/effect allowlist, seeded `always_true`/`noop` — anything else fails closed, proven via a registered-but-unimplemented `never_true` guard that blocks a real transition attempt with `guard_not_implemented` rather than silently passing), `app.validate_workflow_definition()` (the publish-time structural gate — all 7 distinct failure modes proven individually: missing states, invalid initial/terminal state, invalid transition from/to, unknown guard/effect, an unreachable state via bounded BFS, a dead-end non-terminal state), `app.publish_workflow_definition()` (composes `PLT-121`'s own `app.publish_config_version()`), and the runtime `app.workflow_instances`/`app.workflow_transition_history` tables with idempotent start / optimistic-concurrency transition / cancel / authority-gated history-read lifecycle functions. A running instance binds the exact published `config_version_id` snapshot at start time — a later republish creates a brand-new version and never mutates it, per `PLT-121`'s own discipline. Definition admin and instance transition/view are separately permissioned (`app.check_config_object_authority()` vs. `app.check_workflow_instance_authority()`), proven directly — a regular `org_user` is denied publishing a definition but can start/transition/cancel their own tenant's instances. The one safe synthetic example (`draft -> submitted -> approved|rejected`) is built and proven entirely inside `scripts/db-tests/workflow.sql`, not seeded into the migration. **Two real defects caught and fixed before any gate ran**: a stray, prematurely-placed `comment on function` statement that would have broken migration application outright, and a missing `SECURITY DEFINER` on `app.get_workflow_instance_history()` — the same privilege-model class `ERR-2026-004` found in `app.mask_email()` and `PLT-121` found in its own resolver functions. `node:test` 472/472, `db:test` 235 total scenario groups, 10/11 gates green — `test:e2e` `NOT_RUN` in this sandbox (same disclosed Playwright browser-binary revision skew as `PLT-117..121`). **The user's "lanjut sd prompt 126" range remains open — this was the first of five tasks it authorizes (Prompts 122–126); the next runtime agent should continue sequentially into `CG-S6-PLT-020` without requesting fresh authorization.** Full detail: `docs/build-log/phase-01/PLT-122.md`.
- **Phase 1 Prompt 121** (`CG-S6-PLT-018`): Configuration Engine — `app.config_types`/`app.config_objects`/`app.config_versions`/`app.config_items`/`app.config_dependencies` implement the shared versioned configuration foundation Tech Arch §13 specifies. 10 sourced Phase-1 config types (`workflow`/`approval`/`status`/`numbering`/`form`/`field`/`notification`/`feature`/`branding`/`terminology`) seeded empty/template only, per `docs/architecture/07_CONFIGURATION_ENGINE_WORKSTREAM.md` §13's own Module Adoption Map — no fabricated rule/approval content. `app.resolve_config()` is the real 6-level override precedence resolver (user→role→branch→company→tenant→global), proven directly with a role-level override beating a tenant-level default and an unconfigured config_type resolving to zero rows (no fabricated universal default, unlike `PLT-117`/`118`/`119`'s evaluators — configuration content itself has no honest default this early). `app.detect_config_dependency_cycle()` is a real, bounded (≤20 hop) recursive-CTE cycle detector gating `app.publish_config_version()`, proven against both a genuine A→B→C→A cycle (blocks) and a non-circular chain (publishes). `app.verify_config_version_current()` is `EXC-CFG-001`'s concrete "stale configuration version" mechanism, proven to flip `true`→`false` across a real intervening publish. The Tech Arch §13.3 8-state lifecycle is deliberately condensed onto this repository's own established draft/published/archived 3-state pattern (`PLT-111`/`117`/`119`) rather than a one-off state machine — full mapping disclosed in the migration's own header. `config_items.canonical_ref` reuses `PLT-119`'s `app.canonical_terms` directly. **One real defect was caught and fixed before any gate ran** (not a post-hoc finding): `app.resolve_config()`/`app.verify_config_version_current()` initially lacked `SECURITY DEFINER`, which would have made both fail "permission denied" for any real `authenticated` caller — the exact same privilege-model mistake class `ERR-2026-004` found in `app.mask_email()`, caught this time during self-review rather than a failed test. `node:test` 457/457, `db:test` 225 scenario groups, 10/11 gates green — `test:e2e` `NOT_RUN` in this sandbox (same disclosed Playwright browser-binary revision skew as `PLT-117..120`). Full detail: `docs/build-log/phase-01/PLT-121.md`.
- **Phase 1 Prompt 120** (`CG-S6-PLT-017`): Master Data Foundation — `app.master_types`/`app.master_records` form a reusable master-data registry (global/tenant scope, stable codes, bounded plain-text aliases, optimistic-concurrency versioning), seeded with exactly one real, sourced initial type: `vendor_rate` (tenant-scoped, `PRC`-owned), directly matching `docs/architecture/05_DATABASE_SCHEMA_WORKSTREAM.md` §4/§11's `ADR-CAND-ARCH-001` resolution — no sample data seeded, only the type definition itself ("empty, no Procurement UI yet"). `app.merge_master_records()` is the merge-safe dedupe model Prompt 120 §24 requires: the source row is never deleted, only marked `canonical_status='merged'` and permanently pointed at its survivor via `merged_into_id`, with its former code/aliases folded into the target's alias list. `app.resolve_master_record()` resolves a code *or* alias to its live canonical record, transparently following a bounded (≤10 hop) merge chain — resolving a since-merged code lands on the live survivor, not a dead row, proven directly. `app.master_records`' RLS policy is the first in this repository to add a global-visibility branch (`tenant_id is null OR has_active_tenant_membership() OR is_supreme_admin()`) alongside strict per-tenant isolation — every prior tenant-scoped table denied cross-tenant visibility outright; this one deliberately allows shared/global reference rows to be seen by every tenant while keeping tenant-scoped rows isolated, proven with real cross-tenant search/resolve negative tests. Adopts `PLT-118`'s `ERR-2026-004` per-migration convention, proven correct. `node:test` 439/439, `db:test` 212 scenario groups, 10/11 gates green — `test:e2e` `NOT_RUN` in this sandbox (same disclosed Playwright browser-binary revision skew as `PLT-117`/`118`/`119`). **This was the final task in the user's explicitly requested "lanjut sd prompt 120" range — no further task should begin without new explicit user authorization.** Full detail: `docs/build-log/phase-01/PLT-120.md`.
- **Phase 1 Prompt 119** (`CG-S6-PLT-016`): Localization — `app.tenant_locale_versions` is a versioned tenant locale/timezone/currency-display default and terminology-override configuration (draft→published→archived, mirroring `PLT-117`'s `app.tenant_brand_versions` exactly). `app.canonical_terms` seeds 24 real terminology codes sourced directly from this repository's own already-shipped `CHECK`-constrained enums (`PLT-105`'s tenant statuses, `PLT-108`'s principal layers, `PLT-109`'s org-unit types, `PLT-110`'s user statuses, `PLT-117`'s brand-version statuses, `PLT-118`'s domain statuses) — not an open-ended key space; a terminology override for an undefined code is rejected outright. `app.resolve_locale_context()` implements the real three-tier fallback Prompt 119 §22 requires: a per-user preference (three new nullable `CHECK`-constrained columns added to `PLT-110`'s already-shipped `app.users` via additive `ALTER TABLE`) → the tenant's published config → the Indonesia-first platform default (`id`/`Asia/Jakarta`/`IDR`, a reasoned inference from ratified RPD-016 sequencing, explicitly distinguished from `DESIGN_SYSTEM.md` §3's still-genuinely-open CargoGrid brand-color decision) — each field (locale/timezone/currency) resolved independently, proven directly (a user can override just their own timezone while still inheriting the tenant's locale/currency). **Currency here is display-formatting metadata only, never exchange-rate/financial-conversion logic** — a dedicated Phase 4 capability (`M-194`, `194_CURRENCY_EXCHANGE_RATE_PROMPT.md`) owns real currency/FX control, disclosed rather than duplicated. This migration adopts `PLT-118`'s new `ERR-2026-004` per-migration convention (explicit `REVOKE EXECUTE ... FROM PUBLIC`), proven to hold correctly. `node:test` 424/424, `db:test` 199 scenario groups, 10/11 gates green — `test:e2e` `NOT_RUN` in this sandbox (same disclosed Playwright browser-binary revision skew as `PLT-117`/`118`). Full detail: `docs/build-log/phase-01/PLT-119.md`.
- **Phase 1 Prompt 118** (`CG-S6-PLT-015`): Custom Domain — `app.tenant_custom_domains` models request→verify→activate→disable (plus reject/expire), with takeover/rebinding prevention enforced as a real database guarantee: a partial unique index spans the three "claim is live" states (`pending_verification`/`verified`/`active`) for a given hostname, so a second tenant's concurrent claim fails atomically regardless of application-level ordering; once a domain is disabled/rejected/expired, the identical hostname becomes claimable by a different tenant (proven as an explicit rebinding test). `app.verify_tenant_domain()` implements the provider-independent verification interface Prompt 118 §20 asks for (compares an externally-supplied observed DNS TXT value against a stored challenge token; the real DNS lookup itself is disclosed `NOT_RUN`, no job/adapter exists yet). `app.resolve_tenant_by_domain()` is the safe public hostname→tenant resolver, `anon`-granted (needed pre-authentication), returning zero rows unless the domain is active and its tenant is active — structurally never an authorization decision (a bare tenant_id/status pair, nothing RBAC/RLS-shaped). Full Unicode IDN is deliberately out of scope (homograph-attack prevention, disclosed) — only ASCII/punycode hostnames validate. **A real, severe, repository-wide privilege defect (`ERR-2026-004`, Sev-1/Critical) was found and fixed during this checkpoint's authoring**: PostgreSQL grants `EXECUTE` to `PUBLIC` on every function by default, and no migration since `PLT-105` had ever revoked it — meaning every "service_role-only" function across 12 prior migrations (90+ functions: `provision_tenant`, `assign_role`, every audit/support/white-label mutation, and more) was actually directly callable by `anon` this entire time. Fixed via a dedicated corrective migration (`REVOKE EXECUTE ON ALL FUNCTIONS IN SCHEMA app FROM PUBLIC` + `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES` to prevent recurrence), re-verified against the full 185-scenario-group db-test suite; one real casualty (`app.mask_email()`, which had relied on the same default for `authenticated` callers via `app.users_directory`) found and re-granted explicitly. `node:test` 408/408, `db:test` 185 scenario groups, 10/11 gates green — `test:e2e` `NOT_RUN` in this sandbox (same disclosed Playwright browser-binary revision skew as `PLT-117`). Full detail: `docs/build-log/phase-01/PLT-118.md`, `docs/runtime/ERROR_LEDGER.md` `ERR-2026-004`.
- **Phase 1 Prompt 117** (`CG-S6-PLT-014`): White-Label Foundation — `app.tenant_brand_versions` is a versioned draft/publish/rollback tenant brand configuration (mirrors `PLT-111`'s `app.role_versions` shape), covering 4 of RPD-019's 5 tenant-override items (logo, colors, email presentation, document-template references — custom domain is `PLT-118`, next). Every token/asset/template value is structurally validated by `CHECK` constraints (a real WCAG 2.2 contrast-ratio formula implemented in SQL, `app.hex_color_contrast_ratio()`, verified against known reference pairs); publishing enforces a 4.5:1 AA contrast minimum on any set primary color, while a token-free version always publishes (the accessible-default alternative flow). `app.evaluate_tenant_brand()` resolves a tenant's effective brand — its own published version, or the accessible CargoGrid default composed only from `docs/standards/DESIGN_SYSTEM.md` §2.1's one already-sourced neutral-900 reference value, never a fabricated brand color — and is deliberately granted to `anon` as well as `authenticated` (public, pre-authentication presentation data a tenant's login page must render). No bespoke `*_history` table: this is the first capability built after `PLT-116` and routes every lifecycle event (draft/tokens-set/publish/rollback/discard) through the canonical `app.capture_audit_event()` instead. **Two real defects found and fixed during authoring**: an ambiguous-column bug inside `evaluate_tenant_brand()`'s own body (its `RETURNS TABLE` output column `tenant_id` shadowed the real table column of the same name) and a systemic `plpgsql` composite-assignment syntax mistake across this checkpoint's own test file — both caught only by actually running `pnpm run db:test`. No shared UI shell exists anywhere in this repository yet, so §15's UI-application scope is disclosed-deferred to the not-yet-built White-label Studio slice (`09_*.md` §14's own atomic backlog). `node:test` 390/390, `db:test` 169 scenario groups, 10/11 gates green — `test:e2e` `NOT_RUN` in this sandbox (Playwright pins revision 1228, the sandbox's pre-installed browsers are revision 1194, a disclosed environment condition unrelated to this checkpoint's diff). Full detail: `docs/build-log/phase-01/PLT-117.md`.
- **Phase 1 Prompt 116** (`CG-S6-PLT-013`): Audit Trail Foundation — `app.audit_logs` is the canonical, tenant-aware compliance trail (`app.capture_audit_event()`, deterministic redaction reproducing `logger.ts`'s sensitive-key list); `app.query_audit_logs()`/`app.export_audit_logs()` are permission-aware (reusing `PLT-115`'s `app.is_support_grant_authority()`), keyset-paginated, and unconditionally self-log their own invocation ("privileged access itself audited," Prompt 116 §26 — a structural guarantee, since `app.audit_logs` itself carries no grant to `authenticated` at all). RPD-022's Supreme Admin exception is implemented via `app.supreme_admin_mutate_audit_log()`/`app.supreme_admin_delete_audit_log()`, both self-capturing before/after evidence (`06_RLS_RBAC_WORKSTREAM.md` §10 test #9). `PLT-115`'s `revoke_support_access()` kill switch now also emits a canonical audit entry (representative platform-event integration, additive `CREATE OR REPLACE`). **A real, pre-existing cross-tenant PII leak in `PLT-114`'s `app.users_directory` was found and fixed**: a non-`security_invoker` view's RLS posture is its *owner's*, not the caller's, and the migration-applying role has `BYPASSRLS` — so the view silently returned every tenant's users to any authenticated caller, undetected until this checkpoint's own new test data exposed it; fixed with an explicit `WHERE` predicate inside the view plus a permanent regression test added to `PLT-114`'s own test file. `node:test` 373/373, `db:test` 153 scenario groups, all 12 gates green. This continues beyond the user's originally-requested "sd prompt 115" range under the user's explicit `2026-07-16` "lanjut" authorization to proceed. Full detail: `docs/build-log/phase-01/PLT-116.md`.
- **Phase 1 Prompt 115** (`CG-S6-PLT-012`): Support Access and Impersonation Control — `app.support_access_grants`/`sessions`/`events` model a time/purpose-bound support grant lifecycle (request→approve/deny→activate→end/expire/revoke); the `support_access_gated` policy family (`docs/architecture/06_RLS_RBAC_WORKSTREAM.md` §4) is implemented as an additive `OR`-branch on `PLT-113`'s already-shipped `app.has_active_tenant_membership()`, proven in the real database tests to compose correctly with `PLT-114`'s `app.can_access_record()` (a support grant satisfies only the tenant-membership prerequisite, never the ownership/share/customer-scope check — the concrete "layers over, does not bypass" proof, Prompt 115 §26). The kill switch is usable by either Supreme Admin or the target tenant's own `tenant_admin`; sessions require a fresh (≤5 minute) re-authentication timestamp; emergency access requires a recorded higher authority and a structurally shorter expiry cap (2h vs. 24h, `CHECK`-constraint-enforced) plus mandatory Supreme-Admin-only post-review. `deriveSupportSessionBanner()` is the reusable impersonation-banner primitive Prompt 115 §15 asks for. **This is the final prompt in the user's explicitly requested "sd prompt 115" range.** `node:test` 361/361, `db:test` 138 scenario groups, all 12 gates green. Full detail: `docs/build-log/phase-01/PLT-115.md`.

- **Phase 1 Prompt 114** (`CG-S6-PLT-011`): Field-Level and Record-Level Access — `app.can_access_record()` (the exact real function name/pattern `docs/architecture/06_RLS_RBAC_WORKSTREAM.md` §11.2/§11.3 already fixes) evaluates record-scope access via ownership, shared org-unit, or customer-account scope, composed entirely from already-`VERIFIED` primitives (`PLT-108`/`110`/`113`); proven exhaustively by direct call since no business-domain `owner_user_id` table exists yet in Phase 1. `app.users_directory` is the field-masking foundation example — the real PII `email` column is masked unless the caller holds the seeded `HRS:View personal data` permission. **Two real bugs found and fixed during authoring by actually running the tests**: a column-level `REVOKE` is a no-op against a prior table-level `GRANT` (Postgres ACLs are additive, not layered) — fixed via table-level revoke + explicit column re-grant, proven empirically against a disposable debug database first; and a view calling a `SECURITY INVOKER` function needs the querying role to hold every privilege that function needs internally — fixed with a narrow `SECURITY DEFINER` wrapper rather than widening `PLT-113`'s deliberate catalogue-table scope decision. Writes remain fully denied for `authenticated` (all writes stay RPC-mediated); `anon` unaffected. `*_history` audit tables and global catalogues deliberately deferred to `PLT-116`. `node:test` 337/337 (unchanged — no new TS surface), `db:test` 96 scenario groups, all 12 gates green. Full detail: `docs/build-log/phase-01/PLT-113.md`.

The Supreme Admin exception (RPD-022) bypasses the lookup entirely; multiple granted roles combine additively (there is no explicit-deny role concept). `server/policies/permission-check.ts` is the shared guard at the exact module path `docs/architecture/06_RLS_RBAC_WORKSTREAM.md` §3 already names; `RbacDecisionCache` mirrors `PLT-106`'s explicit-invalidation pattern as a deliberately separate cache. `node:test` 337/337, `db:test` 84 scenario groups, all 12 gates green. Full detail: `docs/build-log/phase-01/PLT-112.md`.
- **Phase 1 Prompt 111** (`CG-S6-PLT-008`): Role and Permission Builder — `app.permissions` is a real, sourced catalogue (64 rows: the 19 permission actions verbatim from `docs/architecture/06_RLS_RBAC_WORKSTREAM.md` §5.1 crossed with `PLT-106`'s real 9-module catalogue, not a fabricated resource taxonomy); `protected=true` matches §5.2's field-masking-gated actions exactly. `app.roles`/`app.role_versions` implement draft→publish→archive with bindings immutable once published (publishing archives the prior published version, the same supersession pattern `PLT-106` established); roles are never seeded. `app.assign_role()` carries a real, proven self-escalation guard — an actor cannot assign themselves a role version carrying a protected permission, using a distinct real-identity actor parameter rather than a spoofable text field. RBAC *enforcement* against these bindings is explicitly deferred to `PLT-112`, next. `node:test` 328/328, `db:test` 72 scenario groups, all 12 gates green. Full detail: `docs/build-log/phase-01/PLT-111.md`.
- **Phase 1 Prompt 110** (`CG-S6-PLT-007`): User Lifecycle — `app.users` layers a tenant-scoped profile (display name, org assignment, a richer invite/active/suspend/revoke lifecycle) on top of `PLT-107`'s identity link and `PLT-108`'s principal memberships, keeping all three in sync rather than letting them drift; `app.invite_user()` composes `app.link_auth_identity()`. `app.transition_user_status()` carries two real, proven integration behaviors: a last-critical-admin guard blocking suspend/revoke of a tenant's only active `tenant_admin`, and a revoke cascade that revokes the underlying identity link and every active principal membership — genuine downstream access cleanup, not new role/permission logic. Invite-by-email-before-signup and live session/token revocation remain disclosed `NOT_RUN` (no live Supabase project, same constraint `PLT-107` already recorded). `node:test` 314/314, `db:test` 60 scenario groups, all 12 gates green. Full detail: `docs/build-log/phase-01/PLT-110.md`.
- **Phase 1 Prompt 109** (`CG-S6-PLT-006`): Organization and Operational Hierarchy — `app.org_units` models tenant-scoped company/branch/department/business_unit nodes with a materialized `path uuid[]` ancestry column (GIN-indexed, no recursive query for ancestor/descendant reads) and a single trigger enforcing the allowed-parent-type matrix, cross-tenant-parent rejection, and cycle prevention together; `app.move_org_unit()` cascades path/depth to every descendant in one bounded update, proven against a 3-level tree. All mutating functions carry real optimistic concurrency (`expected_version`), rejecting a stale value outright — the first Platform Core capability to implement this (`PLT-105`–`108` only passively incremented `record_version`). Deactivation is blocked while any active child exists; nodes are never hard-deleted. The allowed-parent-type matrix is this checkpoint's own disclosed construction (no ratified document fixes one beyond a flat four-type list). `node:test` 304/304, `db:test` 47 scenario groups, all 12 gates green. Full detail: `docs/build-log/phase-01/PLT-109.md`.
- **Phase 1 Prompt 107** (`CG-S6-PLT-004`): Supabase Auth Integration — `app.tenant_user_identities` links `auth.users.id` to `app.tenants` (idempotent invite, guarded revoke, multi-tenant-per-identity and cross-tenant isolation both proven for real); `lib/auth/session-cookie-options.ts`/`redirect-allowlist.ts` (pure, tested secure-cookie and open-redirect-defense primitives). Live GoTrue server-client/middleware/email/session-refresh/MFA-challenge wiring is disclosed `NOT_RUN` — no live Supabase project exists (`ADR-0010`), matching `SECURITY_STANDARDS.md` §1's disclosure discipline rather than fabricating an untestable integration. A local-only `auth.users` test fixture (`scripts/db-tests/fixtures/`, never a real migration) makes the linkage logic provable today regardless. `node:test` 282/282, `db:test` 26 scenario groups, all 12 gates green. Full detail: `docs/build-log/phase-01/PLT-107.md`.
- **Phase 1 Prompt 106** (`CG-S6-PLT-003`): Subscription/Module/Feature Entitlement — versioned package/module/feature entitlement model (`app.entitlement_packages`/`app.tenant_entitlements`/`app.tenant_entitlement_overrides`), catalogue seeded from real `01_MODULE_DEPENDENCY_MAP.md` §3.2 data (9 modules, 41 features), fail-closed `evaluate_entitlement()` (stage 1 of the 8-stage access flow), `server/queries/entitlement.ts` with an explicit-invalidation cache proven end-to-end against `server/mutations/entitlement.ts`. `node:test` 261/261, `db:test` 19 scenario groups, all 12 gates green. Full detail: `docs/build-log/phase-01/PLT-106.md`.
- **Phase 1 Prompt 105** (`CG-S6-PLT-002`): Tenant Provisioning and Lifecycle — **the first Platform Core capability implementation and the first real migration/`server/` code in this repository.** `supabase/migrations/20260716075355_create_tenants.sql`: `app.tenants`/`app.tenant_status_history`, trigger-enforced canonical lifecycle (`provisioning→active→suspended→active→terminated`, terminal-state and legal-hold guards), idempotent `provision_tenant()`/guarded `transition_tenant_status()` RPCs, RLS enabled with schema-privilege defense in depth (`anon`/`authenticated` denied before RLS is even evaluated; `service_role` explicitly granted — BYPASSRLS alone does not bypass GRANT-based checks, proven for real). `server/contracts/tenant/tenant.ts` + `server/mutations/tenant.ts` (dependency-injected client, typed errors, no live Supabase project needed yet). Proven against a real, disposable local Postgres database — 8 scenario groups (idempotency, duplicate-slug rejection, full lifecycle, terminal-state/invalid-transition/legal-hold rejection, non-existent-tenant handling, RLS/privilege isolation) — via a new `pnpm run db:test` gate, wired into a new CI `db` job with a real `postgres:17` service container. `node:test` 248/248, all 12 gates green (11 carried + `db:test`). Full detail: `docs/build-log/phase-01/PLT-105.md`.
